#2970. Celtics make surprising starting lineup decision against Bucks

MILWAUKEE — Mike Conley will get his first start of the season when the Celtics face the Milwaukee Bucks on Sunday night. Conley, who has mostly been out of the rotation, played a season-high 11 minutes on Friday night against the Charlotte Hornets.

In his 20th NBA season, Conley is averaging career-lows across the board; he's tallying just 3 points and 0.3 assists in 7 minutes per game across three appearances. But, on Sunday night, he'll get a rare opportunity to see more extended minutes.

Conley, who has been a starter in the NBA for the majority of his career, told CelticsBlog before the season that he knew mentorship would be one of his important responsibilities while his on-court minutes vary.

"It's part of the job at this stage of my career," he said. "I understand it. I understand there's going to be a lot of times where I don't play. There's going to be some times where I'm needed. There are other times where I'm just needed to be the voice for a big stretch of the season. Being comfortable with that, and being able to own that, is my role and my job."

And, while Conley hasn't played much so far, he has been a crucial voice in the locker room, particularly to Payton Pritchard, who has routinely asked him questions throughout the season.

"As somebody that's looked up to Mike, [I ask him] how he thinks of the game and how he was able to create his shots and create shots for his teammates and maybe handle playing the point guard position, momentum swings," Pritchard said. "He's been in a lot of great environments, great playoff series. How was he able to kill in those moments and dominate the game?"

The Celtics' most popular starting lineup this season has been Derrick White, Jordan Walsh, Paul George, Jayson Tatum, and Neemias Queta. That group started Friday night in a 119-80 win over the Charlotte Hornets.

But Joe Mazzulla has tinkered with the starting lineup quite a bit; Hugo Gonzalez started 3 games in place of Walsh, while Sam Hauser and Baylor Scheierman have each gotten one start this season. Mitchell Robinson started the first five games of the season before Queta established himself as the team's starting center.

The three guys who have mostly found themselves out of the regular rotation are Conley, Chris Cenac Jr., and Ron Harper Jr. Cenac Jr. is currently with the Maine Celtics, and Conley will get his first start of the season.

#2968. Jessie Bates trade grades as Jaguars-Falcons swap star safety for draft picks

The NFL trade deadline is just over 24 hours away.

And Monday morning delivered the latest big deal as teams try and improve ahead of the push for the playoffs.

The Jacksonville Jaguars made a move to shore up the back of the secondary, pulling off a trade for safety Jessie Bates III of the Atlanta Falcons. Here are the full terms of the deal:

Falcons receive: 2027 second-round pick and a 2028 fifth-round pick

The Jaguars look like one of the NFL's elite teams.

Heading into Week 10, Jacksonville sits at 6-2, three games ahead of the Houston Texans in the AFC South. The Jaguars are a likely playoff team, and have some metrics to back up their elite status. Jacksonville is tops in the NFL with a point differential of +108, and when you look at "Team Tiers," which examines both offenses and defenses through the lens of Expected Points Added, the Jaguars are on par with the San Francisco 49ers at the head of the pack:

So it is probably no surprise that general manager James Gladstone is going to be aggressive ahead of the NFL trade deadline. With the Jaguars in a clear window to contend, making a move like this makes a good deal of sense. And word out of Jacksonville in recent days held that the Jaguars were looking for "defensive playmakers," and Bates certainly fits that description.

And with Atlanta paying most of the contract, that helps put this deal in a more favorable light for Jacksonville.

What brings the grade down a bit is the contract situation. Bates is in the final year of his contract, and is set to be an unrestricted free agent in 2027. This could mean he is a one-year rental for the Jaguars, which has to factor into the equation. Now, if Jacksonville were to sign him to a new deal, that would certainly bump the grade up a bit, but the contact has to play a role in the equation here.

Jaguars grade: B (which can improve with some extra credit, i.e., signing Bates to an extension).

On the one hand, it is something of a head-scratcher to see Atlanta trade away a key defensive player, given the fact that the Falcons are still in the thick of things in the NFC South playoff race.

If the NFC South playoff race could be described as such.

Atlanta sits at 3-6, at the bottom of the division. But they are not far behind the first-place Carolina Panthers (who clock in at 3-5) and with a win over the Panthers already this season, there is a case that Atlanta should be buying rather than selling.

But with Bates set to hit free agency, this might be the best opportunity to get something for him beyond a compensatory selection, and a second-round pick in 2027 and a fifth-round pick in 2028 is a nice little return. And there is also a chance that the Falcons would not get a compensatory pick, given the fact that Atlanta is set to have over $100 million in cap space next free agency cycle. If the Falcons are active enough in free agency, that would cancel out any compensatory selections, so getting picks now in hand is also a solid bit of business.

ESPN predicted over the weekend that a trade for Bates would cost a third-round pick and a sixth-round pick, so it seems Atlanta improved on that scenario as well.

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2953. The midterm election could reshape the muni bond market. Here’s what investors need to know

The outcome of Tuesday's election is expected to have broad implications across the market, including municipal bonds.

The spotlight has been on the battle for congressional control and whether Democrats will wrest the House of Representatives, the Senate or both from Republicans. But for the muni market, what happens at the state level could have bigger repercussions — at least in the near term.

There is an array of initiatives on ballots around the nation that could change tax laws and add to bond supply. Taxes matter to muni investors since the income received is free of federal tax, as well as state tax if the holder lives in the state where the bond is issued.

Depending on the location, ballot measures are up for approval that would either provide tax breaks, or raise taxes on the wealthy.

"It's a complete bifurcation between red and blue states," said Dan Close, head of municipals at Nuveen. "Blue states are trying to put in place graduated income tax structures, trying to capture more revenue from high-income earners. Red states are promoting affordability by trying to cut property taxes."

Red states are those that predominantly vote Republican, while blue states swing toward Democrats.

For instance, California has a proposition known as the "billionaire tax," which would impose a one-time levy equal to 5% of the wealth of certain taxpayers with at least $1 billion in assets. The state also has a measure on the ballot that would make a temporary tax hike permanent on individuals earning over $371,000, adjusted annually for inflation. The tax is currently set to expire in 2031.

"The impact to the muni bond market will be to make the exemption more valuable," Close said of the possible passage of tax increases. Investors will likely seek shelter in the muni market, which would send bond prices higher, he noted.

It is a different story in Florida, where voters are set to decide whether to increase the Sunshine State's homestead exemption for non-school district property taxes. Qualified homeowners currently have a $25,000 property tax exemption, as well as another for $25,000 that doesn't apply to school property taxes. The new proposal raises the latter exemption to $150,000 in 2027 and $250,000 by 2028. New residents have a five-year waiting period before receiving the benefit.

If passed, some local governments will have to find other revenue sources or increase the overall tax rate to replace what they've lost, said Tom Kozlik, head of public policy and municipal strategy at HilltopSecurities.

"It's not necessarily going to result in downgrades for sure, but there would have to be some balancing with regard to overall revenues," he said.

Not all municipalities will be hurt equally, which means credit research is important, said Close.

"Like everything in municipal finance, it is really on a case-by-case basis that you could have some credit deterioration from [a] rollback of property taxes," he said.

Other voters on Tuesday will be asked to approve more state or local government debt. That could weigh on a market that has already seen heightened supply.

Some $473.8 billion has already been issued year to date, through September, according to the Securities Industry and Financial Markets Association. That's a 7.8% increase over the same period a year ago. In 2025, total muni issuance reached $587.3 billion, 14.3% higher than 2024, Sifma said.

Cooper Howard, director of fixed income research and strategy at the Schwab Center for Financial Research, believes supply is the biggest issue coming out of the election.

"That amount of issuance has been one of the reasons why you have to see that strong amount of demand continue to keep pace with it for total returns to keep up," Howard said.

Fortunately, new supply doesn't necessarily come quickly into the market. "There's a window over a couple years that they can issue," he noted.

The ultimate makeup of the next Congress could also have consequences for muni investors as lawmakers sort out their priorities. Most notable is what lawmakers will do to tackle the country's affordability problem, Kozlik at Hilltop said.

"There could be some tax policy initiatives and even affordability-related initiatives that manifest themselves in the first quarter of next year," he said. "That could impact housing. It could impact higher education. It could impact public power financing."

Investors considering moves before the election may want to look at those states with tax increases on the ballot, said Close at Nuveen.

For instance, Nuveen recently began offering a separately managed account dedicated to Washington after getting an enormous number of inquiries from financial advisors. The state recently enacted a 9.9% tax on individuals with taxable annual income above $1 million, set to go into effect in 2028. But the issue is now being put to voters, who can decide whether to repeal the new law.

"At the margin, if you do think that some of these measures are going to pass, knowing that bonds usually move up with an increase in taxes, there might be some opportunities to buy ahead of the election for blue states," Close said.

Howard believes munis are attractive right now, particularly for those who are in a tax bracket of 24% or above. Fundamentals are good and the bonds largely have good credit quality, he said. "About 70% of the broad muni index is either triple-A or double-A rated."

Kozlik is also bullish, calling yields "really attractive." He suggests investors concentrate on AA-rated or higher bonds.

"The muni space is part of the portfolio that most investors want," he said. "It helps them sleep well at night."

Austin Reaves having words with a fan during the Lakers' road game against the Pistons. Khobi Price

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

Week 9 NFL Takeaways: Why the Packers Are Looking Like Super Bowl Contenders – SI

The Eagles' reluctance to deal Moro Ojomo, and buzz that they want to eventually sign him long term, has people in the league wondering if they'd eventually trade Jalen Carter. At this point, it's just speculation. But there's good logic there, too (they're already paying two guys, Carter and Jordan Davis, at Ojomo's position). It was interesting, too, that Nick Sirianni didn't shoot down the possibility out of hand that he'd make staff changes after Sunday's loss.

10 Eagles things I think I think after the loss to the Giants – BGN

1) Someone on this coaching staff needs to be let go during the bye week. The Eagles have to make a significant move on this coaching staff, particularly on the offensive side of the ball. What this offense is doing in the second half of games is unacceptable. The Eagles had 99 total yards in the second half, scoring just three points. They had zero total yards in the third quarter, running 9 plays and going 3-and-out on all three possessions in the period. Philadelphia is the only team averaging under 2.0 points and 50 yards in the third quarter this season. Doesn't matter who the quarterback is. The system remains the same, inefficient and ineffective. The offensive line allowed seven sacks, and had Joe Milton under duress throughout the day. They continue to have miscommunication up front, missing assignments and gaps in run blocking and allowing too many free rushers in pass protection.

Roob's Observations as Eagles crumble in ugly second half against Giants – NBCSP

I don't even know what to say anymore about the Eagles' offensive line. They knew the Giants were going to blitz constantly and the Giants did and the Eagles just weren't prepared for it. They couldn't handle it. Everybody knows the Giants blitz more than anybody in the league other than the Vikings and still they weren't ready for it. Seven sacks? They weren't all on blitzes, but the Giants' defensive line – which had just nine sacks all year coming into this game – nearly matched that in one game. This o-line can't block anybody. It's just embarrassing. The Eagles have now allowed 12 sacks in their last two games, the most ever in a two-game span under Nick Sirianni and second most in the last 25 years. They've allowed 30 sacks in nine games, which is insane. And they can't run the ball, either. I don't know if there are any answers. There's nobody to bench, no young guy who needs to play and Jeff Stoutland isn't walking through that door. This may be the worst offensive line in the NFL and I don't know if there's any way for it to get better.

Handing out 10 awards from the Eagles-Giants game – PhillyVoice

2) The 'Same Old, Same Old' Award: The Eagles' offense. The Eagles had just 220 yards of total offense. 13 first downs. A paltry 3.3 yards per play. And they were on the field nine and a half fewer minutes than the Giants. The offense has been awful all season, the coaching staff has no answers, etc. I feel like I can prewrite this award every week and just fill in the numbers.

But we need to see some of the sloppiness go away. Teams that play dumb and make this many mistakes are not well coached. Sirianni needs to find some answers if he wants to be back in 2027. That doesn't mean a bunch of wins. He needs the team to play with better situational awareness and show that they understand the little things. That would show that he knows how to solve problems and has the right messaging to get things fixed. If Sirianni can't get the 2026 Eagles to play smarter, do you want him coaching the 2027 Eagles? The bye week is here. Identify problems. Find solutions. Get this team to play better. Or else.

NFL Week 9: Big takeaways, questions and fantasy spin from Sunday's games – ESPN

What to make of the QB performance: Joe Milton got the start despite being with the team for only three weeks. Predictably, the operation was rough around the edges. Milton was able to push the ball downfield at times, and his mobility helped extend some plays. But second-half struggles continued despite the change from Tanner McKee to Milton — as did the leaks along the offensive front. The fact that the story remains the same with a different signal-caller points in large part back to coaching.What to make of the QB performance: Joe Milton got the start despite being with the team for only three weeks. Predictably, the operation was rough around the edges. Milton was able to push the ball downfield at times, and his mobility helped extend some plays. But second-half struggles continued despite the change from Tanner McKee to Milton — as did the leaks along the offensive front. The fact that the story remains the same with a different signal-caller points in large part back to coaching.

How different will Eagles' roster be after NFL trade deadline and Week 10 bye? – The Athletic

Then there's the coaching staff. The Eagles have the worst offense in the NFL. Offensive coordinators have been dismissed in recent years having overseen far better production. Perhaps the Eagles wonder how the offense will finish when DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks return and Saquon Barkley is healthier. But it's hard to view the problems as entirely personnel-related. The Eagles seem flummoxed by opposing defenses and lack answers. Their efficiency numbers are so alarming that a change would be merited if Sirianni wanted a new play-caller. Kuper's offensive lines are not only struggling in the unavoidable shadow of his predecessor Jeff Stoutland, they're struggling as merely a functional NFL group.

2026 NFL season, Week 9: What We Learned from Sunday's games – NFL.com

Milton can't jump-start Eagles' offense. Philly's offense remains a slog, even with the athletic Joe Milton III under center. The QB added a few good runs, leading the Eagles with 64 yards on nine rushes. Milton completed 18 of 32 passes for 169 yards while taking seven sacks. Milton owns a whip, but the issue is his accuracy. Too often, the young QB was off the mark, leading to stalled drives. The Eagles, who entered Sunday with a league-high 34.1% three-and-out rate, went three-and-out seven times on 13 drives. Philly didn't get a first down on three third-down drives. As it has all season, offensive line issues have exacerbated the problems. There were very few open holes in the run game, and far too many free rushers at the QB. Saquon Barkley generated just 22 yards on 13 carries (1.7 yards per carry) with a TD. That's not enough to help a young QB in his first start.

Nick Sirianni: We'll talk about possible staff changes during bye week – PFT

That's shifted the focus in Philadelphia to what kinds of changes may be coming for the team. Head coach Nick Sirianni was asked if there's any chance of those changes starting with alterations to the coaching staff during the week off. "We'll talk. Obviously, right now my job is to be there for our guys and to go watch the tape and go look through everything," Sirianni said, via a transcript from the team. "I think we always think — we'll do everything. I'll do anything I can to help this team win football games. Like I said, I'll look at myself internally first and then go from there. My job is to coach the players and to coach the coaches, and anything I can do to help this team win games, we'll do. Everything can always be on the table, but I don't think you change just to change because that's what your record says that — you've got to look at what's really real and not react to anything else. Again, I just don't believe you just change just to change. If you have a strong reason to do things in anything, not just change, but in anything and you're convicted on it, that's how we'll go about our business."

Game Recap: Giants 19, Eagles 11 – PhiladelphiaEagles.com

The Eagles' offense continued their season-long struggles despite the quarterback change, losing to the Giants 19-11 at Lincoln Financial Field. Philadelphia falls to 2-7 on the season as the Giants improve to 5-3-1, controlling the NFC East. The Eagles now head into a bye week in Week 10. The NFC East matchup was dictated by defensive line play from both teams. Inside linebacker Arvell Reese was the storyline of the game. The rookie dominated the game for the Giants, sacking Joe Milton three times while blitzing and led the game in tackles (12). Edge rusher Kayvon Thibodeaux got the better of rookie tackle Markel Bell throughout the game, pressuring and hitting Milton multiple times. "It's hard to be positive when you play like that," Jordan Mailata said after the game. "I think teams are doing a really good job of blitzing us in the run game, creating heavy boxes, which they don't appear to be heavy… you can't cut it back because there's a guy unblocked."

Jim Harbaugh is a clown and the Chargers are his circus – SB Nation

Winner: The Giants for making a statement. It's not exactly an accomplishment beating the Eagles this season — but beating Philly was critical in the ongoing mission for the Giants to prove they can take the next step to take over the NFC East. It wasn't always pretty for the G-Men in The Linc, with both teams struggling on offense and it being mostly a defensive affair, but this was a very John Harbaugh football game and showed the edge he's brought to the Giants since arriving. It remains to be seen whether or not J.J. McCarthy is good enough to really lead the Giants, but he's playing good enough football where he can take care of the ball, make some solid throws, and most importantly, not give away games. McCarthy also earned some serious locker room points by playing through the pain after taking a big hit, and continuing to play despite taking a big hit to his ribs. There's a lot of football left, but the Giants have the momentum right now. As we approach the trade deadline it's the Giants who I'm looking closely at. Perhaps no other team could benefit more from going in and making a deal for Jonathan Taylor than this one. I understand there are thousands of Cam Skattebo fans, but between his general inconsistency and routine injury issues, it doesn't make sense to saddle so much of the organization's hopes to him. Getting a legitimate three-down back like Taylor would take pressure off McCarthy, open up the play-action pass, and really bring another element to this team.

3 things we learned about the Cowboys from the Colts game – Blogging The Boys

This game had all the makings of a Cowboys blowout. But in classic Cowboys fashion, they allowed the Colts to claw their way back in to take the lead late in the fourth quarter. Thanks to miscues and penalties, the Cowboys failed to cash in on points throughout the day. Still, even through the extremely frustrating performance, the better team did win in the end. The Cowboys didn't make it easy on themselves but did finish the day with a massive touchdown drive thanks to a big play from Dak Prescott to Ryan Flournoy, finished off with a Javonte Williams touchdown. No matter how many yards your offense creates, a mistake-filled performance like this won't net you many wins in the NFL. But for just this week, the Cowboys were able to survive and walk away with the win.

Washington's Loss to the Rams Confirmed What We Already Knew – Hogs Haven

If I told you the Washington Commanders lost a game in which they held the NFL's eighth-best third-down offense to a 21 percent conversion rate, limited the sixth-best red-zone offense to a 50 percent success rate, and forced two turnovers against the league's fourth-best offense while surrendering just 20 points, would you believe me? Yes? Well, I'd believe me, too. When the same mistakes and shortcomings keep plaguing a team week after week, you eventually have to accept that the team is exactly what it has shown you. For the Commanders, that reality has become increasingly difficult to ignore after their 20-13 loss to the Los Angeles Rams, dropping Washington to 2-5-1. Time is running out on Dan Quinn and the Commanders, and the urgency surrounding their season is growing with every passing week.

10 winners, 2 losers as the NY Giants end their 13-game losing streak in Philadelphia – Big Blue View

"Hi, Ed (Dodds, Colts interim GM). Joe here. Forget Jonathan Taylor. What would it take to get Daniel Jones?" OK, only kidding, only kidding. But a ridiculous sequence of events for Giants quarterbacks this season demands an equally ridiculous reaction. In our BBV predictions for this game, I suggested that it would show us whether the Giants are for real or frauds. After today's 19-11 nail-biter over the Philadelphia Eagles, the answer is…some of both? The Giants managed to hang on for their first win at The Linc since 2013 over a not-good Eagles team missing key players on offense, but it wasn't easy. In fact it was a lot like the Giants' last win there, when kicker Josh Brown accounted for all 15 Giants points in a 15-7 victory. Sunday, Jameis Winston did at least get the Giants into the end zone once, but the offense overall was pretty anemic. Still, a win is a win. Let's discuss some things we learned today.

#2939. Austin Reaves leads Luka-less Lakers to road win over Pistons

The Lakers controlled most of Sunday afternoon's game against the Pistons, including withstanding a furious rally in the second half to pick up a big road win, 121-116.

The purple and gold were without Luka Dončić for the contest as he was ruled out on Sunday morning with lower back tightness. Still, the team set the tone from the start and jumped out to a big lead in the first.

Detroit steadily chipped away at the lead before flying back into the contest in the fourth quarter with a big run with Austin Reaves on the bench. From there, it was a tight affair to the final buzzer.

LA finished with 16 turnovers but scored 23 points off Detroit's 13 turnovers. They also outscored the Pistons 58-46 in the paint with many of those points from Detroit coming in garbage time.

LA got off to a solid start with Matisse Thybulle stealing the ball, leading to Austin Reaves converting a layup. Thybulle was also left wide open under the basket for an easy two points. Los Angeles continued their strong start, going on an 8-0 scoring run that featured two triples from Quentin Grimes.

Grimes and Austin Reaves combined for 14 points.

The Lakers built a 14-point lead. Detroit was shooting 26% from the field. LA scored 14 points off the Pistons' seven turnovers.

An incredible quarter ended with Los Angeles getting help from each player who checked in. Sandro Mamukelashvili had five points and Ziaire Williams had eight off the bench. Grimes was in double figures with 10.

Jarred Vanderbilt grabbed six rebounds and dished two dimes. The purple and gold were up by 24 at the end of the first.

Detroit woke up offensively to start the second period. Daniss Jenkins and Kevin Huerter combined for nine points, slowly chipping away at LA's lead. The Pistons had taken over the momentum, shooting 42% from the field, while the Lakers sh-ot 21%.

Los Angeles' 26-point lead dwindled to 12.

Williams was a bright spot for LA. With Luka Dončić out, he and Grimes were stepping up with 11 points each. Los Angeles' suffocating defense from the first quarter had evaporated as they continued to leave Jenkins open for triples.

It was a Jenkins takeover as he ended the quarter with 22 points and was 6-7 from behind the arc. The Lakers weathered the storm enough to lead by 12 at halftime.

Momentum reversed again with LA starting the third period, building on their lead once more on the shoulders of Reaves. Reaves scored 13 points, pushing his total to an insane 32.

Huerter tried to keep the Pistons in it with back-to-back buckets. Cade Cunningham also saw the ball go in the basket, scoring four points.

It was all Reaves, though. He helped put the Lakers up by 17 with 6:30 left in the quarter. Detroit responded with Jenkins and Duncan Robinson each draining triples. The Pistons were outscoring LA 25-23 in the quarter.

Things took an absolute turn for the worse for the purple and gold as they fell apart and allowed Detroit to go on an 11-0 scoring run.

Collin Sexton restored some order for LA, scoring four in a row to put them up by seven at the end of the third.

The final frame was all Pistons early as they tied the game on the shoulders of Jenkins, who continued to shoot ridiculous from behind the arc. He was 8-13 from three and had 28 points.

For Los Angeles, Sexton was feeling it, scoring four points, helping put the Lakers back up. It was a three-point game with 6:59 left.

Crunch time became a back-and-forth affair as the teams traded baskets. Detroit made it a one-point game, but Williams responded by draining a triple. Jake LaRavia further extended the lead with a pair of free throws.

Robinson then made it a three-point game with 3:16 left.

The Lakers answered the call with Reaves, Kessler and Williams taking over the scoring duties, combining for 10 points, including a massive three from Reaves in the final minute.

Sexton had seemingly put the game away with free throws, but fouled Jenkins on the other end from behind the arc. Jenkins made all three free throws to make it a four-point game with 26 seconds left.

Sexton rectified the situation with two free throws. Detroit played the foul game and fouled Reaves, who then iced the win with a pair of free throws.

Reaves finished with 41 points, five rebounds and four assists. Kessler ended with 12 points, 16 rebounds, four assists and three blocks.

Grimes pitched in with 12 points and four assists. Williams had a stellar showing off the bench with 17 points. Vanderbilt did the dirty work with 10 rebounds and three dimes.

Mamu logged seven points. Sexton scored 17 points.

The Lakers' next game will be against the Atlanta Hawks on Monday at 4:00 PM PT.

You can follow Karin on Twitter at @KarinAbcarians.

#2938. Ideal 49ers pass rush target asks his team to trade him

The 49ers' pass rush was not the force many might have expected on Sunday in their 38-17 win over the Las Vegas Raiders.

Though the 49ers forced five turnovers en route to victory, the miscues by the Raiders, for whom quarterback Kirk Cousins threw three interceptions, were not a product of overwhelming pressure from a defensive line boosted by the trade for DeForest Buckner.

Indeed, Cousins was only pressured 31.3% of the time, which was 24th heading into Sunday Night Football in Week 9 among all quarterbacks. Not terrible, but not great for a team once again boasting Buckner's prowess in that area and also getting Nick Bosa back from injury.

The somewhat underwhelming showing underlined why the 49ers may feel the need to add further to their defensive line before Tuesday's trade deadline.

And an ideal target at the edge rusher spot has reportedly requested to be traded, according to NFL Network and ESPN's Ian Rapoport.

Will McDonald IV is projected as an outside pass rusher ideally suited to the wide 9 front often employed by the 49ers when he came out in 2023, a year in which San Francisco did not have a first-round pick.

With an athletic profile that checked all the boxes, explosiveness that was crystal clear on tape, a well-refined pass rush arsenal, and positional versatility, having often played essentially on the interior in Iowa State's 3-3-5 front in college, it was hard to think of a prospect better suited to the 49ers in 2023, and he remains an excellent fit.

Despite the Jets' continued struggles, McDonald has established himself as a consistently productive pass rusher. He has 24.5 sacks for his career, three of which have come this season as New York has slumped to a 2-7 start. McDonald had eight sacks last year and 10.5 in 2024.

Now McDonald, under contract through 2027, wants out of New York, with Rapoport stating that the 27-year-old is unhappy with his usage by the Jets, having been utilized as more of a situational rusher this season.

Rapoport added that teams have called on McDonald, but the Jets have not engaged in trade talks and do not plan to.

However, things can change quickly in the NFL, and the prospect of adding another talented pass rusher under contract for next season should compel the 49ers to test their resolve.

The season-ending ankle injury suffered by rookie defensive tackle Gracen Halton could potentially complicate matters when it comes to the 49ers adding further pieces up front.

But the acquisition of a player like McDonald would be a substantial boost to the edge rotation and enable the likes of Mykel Williams and Keion White to be used more frequently inside and help fill the void left by Halton's injury.

Buckner was a smart, cost-effective addition by the 49ers, but their former 2016 first-round pick should not be the final piece of the puzzle up front. There is a clear need on the edge, and McDonald is the ideal man to fill it.

#2934. In pictures: France protests draw 146,000 as clashes erupt for fourth straight weekend

Protesters clashed with police in France on Saturday as demonstrations over living costs and public services entered a fourth consecutive weekend and drew students, trade unions and members of the revived "Yellow Vest" movement onto the streets.The country's Interior Ministry counted 146,000 demonstrators nationwide, including 46,000 in Paris. A youth union alliance put attendance at 250,000 nationally and 75,000 in the capital. At least 360 people were arrested across the country, AFP reported, citing a ministry tally.

Police used tear gas against people throwing projectiles as the main Paris march ended at Place de la Nation, per AFP. Garbage cans were set alight as protesters and police clashed in several cities, according to Le Monde.

Police blocked a separate, banned Yellow Vest gathering at Place de la Concorde and arrested two movement leaders, Le Monde reported.

The Yellow Vests emerged in 2018 in opposition to fuel-tax increases, before broadening into a movement protesting high living costs and the policies of President Emmanuel Macron.

The turnout was lower than on the previous three weekends. It was also below union-led demonstrations on Nov. 5, when the Interior Ministry counted 575,000 people nationwide.

The protests began in late September with high-school students demanding better conditions, before university students and unions joined.

This weekend's protests come after French lawmakers rejected the revenue section of the 2027 budget, as the government wrestles with addressing public anger while trying to rein in its finances.

The government's original proposal now goes to the Senate, which is scheduled to begin debating it Nov. 20.

Prime Minister Sébastien Lecornu is due to meet labor and employer representatives Monday to discuss wages and purchasing power.

#2931. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2930. How the NFL playoff picture shapes up ahead of Bills-Vikings

Just one game remains on the Week 9 slate in the NFL.

The 6-1 Buffalo Bills head to Minnesota to take on the 6-1 Vikings, in a big test for both teams.

But thanks to results around the league on Sunday, the NFL playoff picture continues to take shape. Here is where things stand in both conferences ahead of Monday night's big game, as well as a look at how Bills-Vikings could alter the state of play in both the AFC and the NFC.

Here are the current playoff standings in the AFC, ahead of Monday night's big matchup between the Vikings and the Bills.

1. Buffalo Bills (6-1)2. Denver Broncos (7-2)3. Jacksonville Jaguars (6-2)4. Pittsburgh Steelers (6-2)5. Kansas City Chiefs (6-2)6. Cincinnati Bengals (6-2)7. New England Patriots (5-4)

Denver would be in as the No. 2 seed.

Monday night's game between the Buffalo Bills and the Minnesota Vikings features a pair of teams at or near the top of their respective conferences.

If the Bills win, Buffalo will improve to 7-1 and remain atop the AFC conference standings. If the Bills lose, while they will drop to 7-2 with the same record as the Broncos, the Bills will stay in the No. 1 spot thanks to a better conference record (4-1 for the Bills vs. 3-1 for the Broncos).

#2928. Trump targets Slotkin in call for DOJ probe of alleged Democratic threats to Republicans

President Donald Trump said Saturday that he was ordering the Justice Department to investigate Democratic leaders over what he described as threats against Republican officials, donors and supporters, without identifying the alleged communications or providing evidence of criminal conduct.

"The Democrats are going around threatening a lot of people, including the Attorney General of the United States, and others, with Impeachment and many other forms of punishment, even though they admit they have no reason to do so," Trump said in a Truth Social post.

The alleged threats, the president said, are "extortion at the highest level," he wrote. "I am ordering the Department of Justice to look into this criminal act, immediately!"

In his post, Trump claimed that Democratic Sen. Elissa Slotkin of Michigan is planning to "go after all young people that believe in Republican Values."

Trump was apparently referencing a speech Slotkin made at a University of Chicago event on Oct. 13. At the time, Slotkin said: "I want young people coming out of school to say, 'Should I go work for the Trump administration?'...I want them to be like, 'Lord, that might be buying myself a ton of problems that I don't want. And so let me avoid participating in that.' I want that deterrence message sent."

Slotkin alleges that she, her family and her staff faced physical and legal intimidation after the posting of a November 2025 video in which Slotkin and other Democratic leaders called on the U.S. military to "refuse illegal orders" following the airstrikes targeting boats in the Caribbean.

On Nov. 20, Trump called the video treason and said "an example must be set."

The FBI contacted lawmakers, including Slotkin, days later. In February, Slotkin declined to sit for an interview the Justice Department requested about the video.

"The intimidation is the point, and I'm not gonna go along with that," the senator said in February. A federal grand jury later declined to indict the video participants.

Now, Trump alleges Democrats are threatening legal intimidation against Republican leaders and their supporters.

"Republicans should get smart, fast! Threatening letters have been sent to many people, all innocent, from Democrat Political Leaders," the president wrote.

The White House and Slotkin didn't immediately respond to a request for comment.

#2927. Sights and Sounds from San Antonio’s Gold Cup early round win at Sacramento

The Spurs put together two of their finest wins during SEGABAGA road victories over Sacramento and Portland. When the NBA schedule came out, my sister and I reviewed which game that we'd want to go to, saw the airfare prices to and from Austin and San Antonio…. and concluded Sacramento made the most sense for both of us.

Some observations from an excellent night in the Golden Center:

The best comp that I could come up with was slightly slower and less athletic Allen Iverson with Jamal Murray's shooting range on offense. However, he and the rest of his teammates seemed lost on most defensive possessions, which resulted in quite a few paint points for San Antonio.

Not only were there wide swaths of the lower bowl that had empty seats, but there was entire rows in the second level that lacked humans, too. Though the Kings did themselves no favors by falling and staying behind by nearly 20 points most of the game, the ambience itself lacked the fervor of past seasons.

Things I saw in-person that I haven't noticed on the good ole television:

Luke Kornet seems 15-20 pounds heavier than last season.

Domantas Sabonis tried a lot of his bull-in-a-china-shop physicality on Wembanyama in the paint – something that worked well in earlier seasons – but found it no longer works for him beyond occasionally.

Dylan Harper (see highlights below) looked like the best guard out there.

Wembanyama suffered his infamous face-plant during game 2 of the first-round series and turned his ankle rather gruesomely on Sabonis' closeout tonight – so maybe I shouldn't be going to these games in-person anymore.

I'm sad I didn't get video evidence of Jordan McLaughlin's dunk attempt in the second half. It was impressive!

Chik-Fil-A rewarded its fans with a free appetizer if a Spur missed two free throws in a row, and this might have been the biggest win tonight in Sacramento…. with the exception that fans had to claim their victory a) in the app and b) before 10 AM the next morning. I'll take breakfast tacos anytime.

What started as a bad omen but ultimately was a big nothing-burger: the Spurs' rim was being repaired for over 20 minutes right after Wembanyama finished his pre-game prep, affecting the tip-off by roughly 15 minutes or so. Right before the repairs took place, I was impressed with Wembanyama's (mostly) in-motion routine instead of just practicing stand-still jumpers:

The audacity AND accuracy of Stephon Castle's re-direction here to Vassell in the first period will likely put this in the season-ending highglight rankings:

Speaking of Devin, this literally was a logo jumper late in the first quarter:

There were shades of young Tony Parker on this and-1 by Harper.

About $90 million of salary failed to box out Wembanyama on this follow slam.

Castle – for my more mature audience – is like a latter generation Dominique Wilkins with these two-footed lob jams on anybody and everybody

These simple two-man motions with Victor and a guard and shooters on the other side are good enough to win a lot of regular season games while keeping both the big man and his teammates happy. Devin's shotmaking ability late in the shotclock is exquisite.

On a personal note, this was the first chance to watch the Spurs in-person with my sister and her two kids since my dad passed. During the last Spurs game here in Sacramento on March 17, my dad suffered his last major fall hurriedly trying to get into his TV room to watch the game. My Kings rep Gavin was kind enough to gift us post-game free throws, and Shirley credited my dad for willing her free throw into the net.

#2926. Why the prognosis for healthcare stocks is uncertain ahead of Congress’ new makeup

Healthcare stocks may struggle to gain momentum as Democrats prepare to take over Congress.

Jared Holz, who covers healthcare for Mizuho, says the outlook for the sector is uncertain because of likely gridlock between lawmakers.

"The next couple of years I really can't see too much happening," the firm's sector specialist told CNBC's "Fast Money" on Wednesday. "I'm not sure you want to own everything, but you can pick and choose the names… Pharma is trading more expensive than tech for the first time in 20 years. So, I backed away from pharma. I would look at medtech."

The State Street Health Care Select ETF (XLV) jumped 3% a day after Election Day.

"You had this very weird situation where typically you wouldn't have all of these categories work in tandem, and they did. So, I think some things just a little bit off," said Holz, a day before healthcare got swept up in Thursday's Wall Street sell-off.

According to Holz, healthcare's real booster shot may come if a Democrat wins the White House in 2028.

"Then I think you see some of the Trump strategies rolled back. That's where I think healthcare could actually perform well," said Holz, who added it hinges on Democrats retaining the Senate and House.

For investors who are long on healthcare, Holz said each subsector should be treated differently.

"Can we get some of the regulation that has taken place over the past couple of years and overturn that over the near term? I don't necessarily believe so," Holz said. "But if you do, then you probably want to be long some of these medical device stocks that are at or near multi-year lows."

The State Street Health Care Select ETF ended virtually flat this week. It's up around 32% since President Trump took office in January 2025 — beating the S&P 500 by almost 9 percentage points.

#2925. Anthropic IPO meets AI backlash after midterms show stinging rebuke of data centers

Americans were unequivocal at the ballot box this week as they expressed disdain for tech billionaires and mistrust of artificial intelligence. With that backdrop, Anthropic is about to try to go public.

The AI company is gearing up for a potentially historic IPO, likely targeting mid-November for its Nasdaq debut. CNBC reported on Friday that Anthropic investors see the company aiming for a valuation of $1 trillion to $1.5 trillion, down from a target of $2 trillion that had been floated in recent months.

From Wall Street to Main Street, it's becoming a tougher environment for Anthropic. The Nasdaq on Thursday suffered its steepest drop in 19 months, and it's down to start November after October marked its worst month since March 2025. Chipmakers and neoclouds, the companies most closely tied to the AI buildout, are getting hit the hardest.

Voters rebuked AI in Tuesday's midterm elections, while sending Democrats to sweeping congressional victories across the country. Candidates who advocated for more stringent AI regulation won handily in states like Michigan, Ohio, Texas and Georgia, which will all now have Democratic governors, and local communities opposed data center-related ballot measures in 40 out of 41 cases.

More than half of voters said AI data centers were a "crucial" or "major factor" in who they supported for Congress, according to an exit poll from NBC News.

"People don't want a data center going near them if it's just going to house AI that's going to help a big tech company profit," Maura Austin, a behavioral scientist at the University of Virginia, told CNBC. "What Anthropic is doing when they're going public with such a high valuation is they're really leaning into that big tech, billionaire kind of caricature. I expect that's not going to go well with the public."

Anthropic has long tried to position itself as a safety-conscious company that takes AI's potential risks and benefits seriously. CEO Dario Amodei co-founded the company alongside a handful of researchers who defected from OpenAI, now its chief rival. Anthropic is best known for developing a family of AI models called Claude, and it primarily sells its products and services to other businesses.

The company's enterprise focus has helped it avoid becoming the public face of AI, leaving that distinction to OpenAI and CEO Sam Altman. But with Anthropic poised to become the first pure-play AI company to hit the public market, minting a number of new billionaires and many more millionaires in the process, a heightened level of scrutiny is coming.

"Being a public company, just that by itself pushes you in the limelight," Reena Aggarwal, a professor of finance at Georgetown University, said in an interview. "And then AI is such a hot-button issue that Anthropic will be in the limelight even more."

Matt Kennedy, a senior IPO market strategist at Renaissance Capital, said that's not necessarily a bad thing for Anthropic.

Public markets force greater transparency, he said, and the company could attract new users through its debut. And since AI has become such a mainstream conversation topic, several private companies are already household names.

"I would say that a public company does not deserve more criticisms than a comparable private company," Kennedy said. "So Anthropic should not be put in a harsher public spotlight than OpenAI just because it's public."

AI backlash accelerated over the summer after a series of high-profile safety and security incidents rattled the public. As concerns mounted, the debate over data centers intensified, while tech's hyperscalers simultaneously boosted their forecasts for capital expenditures, each reaching into the hundreds of billions of dollars.

Anthropic and OpenAI depend on state-of-the-art data centers to train models and serve customers, and they're trying to build out capacity as quickly as possible to meet demand. Anthropic said in an amended IPO prospectus this week that it has entered into purchase commitments and lease agreements with an aggregate committed spend of about $895.4 billion, "substantially all of which is related to compute and infrastructure."

Americans grappling with inflation and stubbornly high gas prices have grown worried that the facilities could exacerbate environmental harms, raise utility bills and contribute to job loss. For many, a proposed data center in their backyard became the physical manifestation of the widespread antipathy toward AI.

In the risk factors section of its prospectus, Anthropic said opposition to data center development has become more organized across jurisdictions, and that the company's public profile could draw particular scrutiny to projects associated with it.

Nicole Greene, an analyst at Gartner, said she expects that Anthropic's executives have long been prepared to contend with AI backlash, especially given the disruptive nature of the technology.

"There's going to be big emotions," Greene told CNBC. "With big emotions and feelings — fear and excitement — comes heightened scrutiny."

Amodei acknowledged the souring sentiment around AI in a post on X in August. He said it's the result of a "crisis of trust," and that people believe companies, the government and the tech industry are always "cooking up some new way to screw them over."

Rebuilding public confidence is going to be an uphill battle as the company begins providing quarterly financial updates.

The University of Southern California published a survey on Monday that found that 71% of respondents don't trust big tech companies to act in society's best interest "at all" around data center development.

"Once you've lost trust, it's hard to gain it back," Wändi Bruine de Bruin, a professor at the University of Southern California's Schaeffer Institute, said in an interview. "The numbers are not good."

#2924. Former Real Madrid goalkeeper urges Mourinho to play Vinicius as a No. 9: ‘Difficult problem to solve’

Real Madrid will be heading into the second international break of the season feeling good about themselves, having strung together eight wins in a row in all competitions.

The team has done exceptionally well, ironically without Vinicius Jr., who had to serve a four-match domestic suspension during this period.

Vinicius will be available at the disposal of Jose Mourinho for La Liga matches when the season resumes later this month.

But there are question marks over how exactly will Vinicius, who is aware that he has lost his place in the starting eleven, will be integrated into Mourinho's first-team plans.

Former Real Madrid goalkeeper Santiago Canizares has a grand solution to that problem: Playing Vinicius as a No. 9.

Can Vinicius thrive as a number nine? (Photo by Diego Souto/Getty Images)

Indeed, in a recent interaction with COPE (h/t Mundo Deportivo), the former goalkeeper has urged Mourinho to start playing Vinicius as the main forward.

"Vinicius is a player who obviously won't go unnoticed; he won't be sidelined," he said. "For me, the solution is for him to play as a number 9, to play in place of Mbappe."

"He's the only possible replacement they have. With both of them on the field, the team can still win games, but it won't have the same identity, the same balance, and the same good vibes," he added.

While Canizares admitted the move will be risky, it is not impossible to see Real Madrid succeed.

Vinicius operating centrally can free up Mbappe even more. (Photo by Clive Brunskill/Getty Images)

"This is a difficult problem to solve. If he can get Vinicius running and integrate him into the team dynamic, that's truly remarkable," he concluded.

Real Madrid can take some amount of inspiration from the fact that Barcelona have thrived without a recognised number nine this season, with Raphinha scoring goals for fun.

If Vinicius can offer the same outfit in the final third, it could only make Real Madrid much more of a threat going forward.

Not to mention, it would free up Kylian Mbappe much more, with the Frenchman already expected to operate in his preferred left-wing role for France during the international break this month.

#2923. What happened to Sean McDermott? Former Bills HC is a top NFL coaching candidate

The Buffalo Bills have been one of the best teams in the NFL entering their Week 9 "Monday Night Football" game against the Minnesota Vikings, posting a 6-1 record to establish themselves as a legitimate Super Bowl contender.

Buffalo has achieved those heights despite making a coaching change ahead of the 2026 NFL season.

The Bills moved on from Sean McDermott after nine seasons with the team. McDermott led the Bills to consistent success but was never able to lead them to a Super Bowl.

Buffalo promoted offensive coordinator Joe Brady from within to help replace McDermott. The 37-year-old is the league's youngest current head coach and has helped the Bills sport one of the league's best offenses amid their torrid start to the season.

Meanwhile, McDermott is still awaiting his next coaching opportunity, and the 52-year-old figures to be one of the top candidates for the 2027 hiring cycle as he seeks an NFL return.

Here's what to know about McDermott's departure from the Bills and where he will rank among the top coaching candidates of the 2027 NFL offseason.

Week 3: Denver Broncos cheerleaders perform during the "Sunday Night Football" game against the Los Angeles Rams at Empower Field at Mile High.

The Bills fired McDermott after the 2025 NFL season, following the team's 33-30 loss to the Denver Broncos in the divisional round of the playoffs.

Bills owner Terry Pegula announced the decision in a statement on Jan. 19.

"Sean has done an [admirable] job of leading our football team for the past nine seasons," the statement read. "But I feel we are in need of a new structure within our leadership to give this organization the best opportunity to take our team to the next level. We owe that to our players and to Bills Mafia."

"Sean helped change the mindset of this organization and was instrumental in the Bills becoming a perennial playoff team," Pegula added. "I respect all the work, loyalty and attention to detail he showed for this team and the community."

Pegula made it clear the Bills' decision to fire McDermott was directly tied to Buffalo's playoff loss to Denver.

"It was where does the leadership of the team, on the field and in the locker room, where [do] we go from that moment, another playoff failure?" Pegula told reporters during a Jan. 21 news conference. "That's why I decided Sean had to leave."

McDermott's firing came on the heels of a 12-5 season during which the Bills made the playoffs for a seventh consecutive season. Many believed Buffalo would have a chance to enjoy a quality postseason run with the Baltimore Ravens, Cincinnati Bengals and Kansas City Chiefs all missing the playoffs, but the Bills only made it to the divisional round.

While McDermott led Buffalo to the playoffs in eight of his nine seasons, he posted an 8-8 record across those postseason appearances. The Bills never made it to the Super Bowl under his tutelage and only twice made it as far as the AFC championship game, which was part of what led Pegula to go in a different direction ahead of the 2026 season.

McDermott posted a 98-50 regular-season record with the Bills across his nine seasons with the team. They only had one losing season across his tenure, but he only posted an 8-8 record during the postseason.

Below is a year-by-year look at McDermott's results with the Bills:

2020: 13-3; lost in AFC championship game

2024: 13-4; lost in AFC championship game

McDermott is not currently coaching, but he is hoping to get another crack at a head coaching job. He made his intentions clear to NFL Media's Rich Eisen in an early-September interview on the host's self-titled radio show.

"I want to coach again," McDermott told Eisen. "I want to be a head coach in the NFL, and I've got this burning desire in my heart to win a world championship, and to take that to an organization and a fan base. I owe that to myself, but I also owe that to the next organization that I work for, because I want to build on the success that we had in Buffalo."

McDermott acknowledged that he was spending his time away from football reflecting on his tenure in Buffalo, assessing what went we well and what he would do differently should he get a second chance.

McDermott also revealed he was talking to members of Bills organization to get their opinions on what he could improve upon.

McDermott should be one of the top coaching candidates on the market during the 2027 NFL offseason. Below is a look at the other top talents who could draw consideration for head coaching roles next season.

Tomlin led the Steelers to a 193-114-2 record across 19 seasons in Pittsburgh and won a Super Bowl while in charge of the franchise. He famously never had a losing season, and his steady performance will certainly endear him to NFL organizations looking for a proven culture-builder to improve their operations.

Tomlin stepped down from the Steelers following the 2025 NFL season and is currently working as a studio analyst for NBC. It isn't yet clear whether he will be willing to return to the sidelines or if he will remain comfortable in the booth for the time being.

JARRETT BELL: Mike Tomlin looms over NFL hiring cycle before it even starts

McDermott's track record of consistency with the Bills should go a long way toward helping him earn a second chance at the NFL level. He's a strong, defensive-minded coach and has shown an ability to bring in quality offensive coordinators, with Brady being among the best hires of his tenure.

McDermott should get plenty of interviews during the 2027 offseason, as his lack of current NFL employment will allow him to interview for any roles that open up during the current season.

The Vikings have gotten off to a 6-1 start to the 2026 NFL season, and it has largely been on the back of Flores' top-rated defense. His aggressive, blitz-heavy attack has helped Minnesota rank second league-wide in defensive EPA per play and first in pressure rate, per the NFL's Next Gen Stats, and the system has proven to be transferrable across all of Flores' NFL stops.

Any NFL organization looking to improve its defense should be interested in bringing Flores aboard as a transformational hire on that side of the ball. But Flores is still embroiled in a discrimination lawsuit against the NFL, and it isn't clear whether that legal process will make any teams apprehensive about hiring him.

Udinski is just 30 years old, but he's a rising star in the coaching ranks. He has helped the Jaguars become one of the NFL's best and most efficient offenses during the 2026 season, and his ability to scheme players open should make him a hot commodity on the coaching market.

Udinski would be one of the NFL's youngest-ever coaches, but NFL owners have been more willing than ever to follow the Sean McVay blueprint and take chances on young coaches. That could give Udinski a path toward earning a coaching job even with just seven years of NFL coaching experience.

GRANT UDINSKI: He drew up plays at 5. At 30, Jaguars OC is one of the NFL's hottest coaches

Doyle is in a similar boat to Udinski. He's just 30 years old, but he has enjoyed a rapid rise over the last two seasons, finding success working as Ben Johnson's offensive coordinator in 2025 before getting a chance to call his own plays with the Ravens this season.

Doyle's candidacy may depend on what he's able to do with the Ravens offense as they navigate injuries to quarterbacks Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley. If Doyle can find success with Jacoby Brissett, that could got a long way toward earning interviews during the upcoming coaching cycle.

#2922. Austin Reaves steps up in Luka Doncic's absence to lead Lakers over Pistons

The Lakers' star guard banked in a fadeaway three-pointer. He nailed a clutch step-back with 44.5 seconds left. He trash-talked with fans.

Reaves embodied Doncic in almost every way Sunday, leading the Lakers to a 121-116 win over the Detroit Pistons as Doncic sat out with low back tightness. The Lakers replaced the guard averaging a league-high 40.1 points with Reaves' season-high 43 points, four assists and five rebounds.

The Lakers (9-2) needed every bit of Reaves' magic. Their 26-point second-quarter lead nearly evaporated under an avalanche of second-half turnovers and 55 combined points from Detroit bench guards Daniss Jenkins and Kevin Huerter.

Fresh off a max contract extension, Reaves has had a quiet yet ruthlessly efficient start to the year in Doncic's shadow. Doncic is getting most valuable player chants each time he steps to the free throw line. Reaves, averaging 25.3 points per game on 50.8% shooting from the field and 41.3% from three, has at times looked clumsy while getting comfortable with new teammates in the minutes without Doncic.

Read more: Luka Doncic's historic run jump-starts early MVP conversation

He looked comfortable in the starring role again Sunday. Reaves hit a seemingly impossible baseline fadeaway in the first quarter, flinging the ball almost over the backboard. When two fans sitting opposite the Lakers' bench started chirping at Reaves, he bent down, put his hands on his knees during a stoppage of play and talked directly at them for several seconds.

Doncic, dressed in black pants and a gray jacket, took it all in from the sideline.

"Sometimes when they're playing together, AR kind of has to take a secondary role," said center Walker Kessler, who had 12 points and 16 rebounds, including a clutch put-back, three-point play that put the Lakers up by eight with 2:15 remaining. "But I mean, as you can see now, he's very capable. He's a heck of a playmaker, heck of a scorer and a competitor."

On the front side of a back-to-back, Doncic missed his second game of the season with low back tightness, although Lakers coach JJ Redick said he didn't anticipate it to affect Doncic significantly throughout the season.

Austin Reaves goes to the basket against Pistons guard Kevin Huerter in the second half Sunday. (Duane Burleson / Associated Press)

The Slovenian superstar is averaging a league-high 40.1 points per game, but Doncic was doing more than just scoring at rates not seen since Wilt Chamberlain. His estimated on/off of 37.7 — the difference between the Lakers' net rating when he was on the court compared to off — was the second-highest in the league, following only Victor Wembanyama's 41.5. In addition to having the highest scoring average through nine games since Chamberlain, Doncic is the team's second-leading rebounder at 7.8 per game and is second with 1.7 steals.

But the Lakers already showed they could compete without Doncic this season. They dominated the Clippers on Oct. 28 when he was sidelined, also with back tightness.

Just as they did against the Clippers, the Lakers needed contributions from everyone. Ziaire Williams hit four three-pointers and finished with 17 points. Collin Sexton, serving as the Lakers' second ball-handler, had 17 points on five-of-10 shooting.

Jarred Vanderbilt's energetic six rebounds, two assists and one block in less than six minutes during the first quarter helped lift the Lakers to a 26-point lead early in the second.

Read more: Swanson: Luka Doncic can lead Lakers to a title, but only if his teammates pull their weight

The Pistons, playing without All-NBA center Jalen Duren (hamstring), didn't get their first points from star point guard Cade Cunningham until early in the third quarter. Instead, Jenkins nearly mounted the comeback on his own.

Jenkins, a familiar Lakers foe who scored 30 points in Detroit's win over the Lakers here last year, scored 22 points of his career-high 31 in the first half, cutting the Lakers' 26-point lead to 13 by halftime. He drained a three-pointer at the buzzer that injected life into the antsy home crowd. The Lakers, who have benefited from two halftime buzzer-beating heaves from Doncic this season, know what it's like to be on the other side of that momentum swing.

Pistons fans banging inflatable thunder sticks erupted in the third quarter as Detroit ripped off an 11-0 run to close the lead to three with 1:03 remaining in the quarter. The Lakers, playing with Sexton as their primary ballhandler during the stretch, had four turnovers during the drought, including a shot clock violation after a timeout.

Reaves rested from the sideline during the slide, but Redick stayed committed to the pre-determined rotation matrix to ensure star players get their necessary breaks.

"If you start just overreacting in-game and saying 'I gotta get this guy back in, I gotta get this guy back in,' it's just chaos," Redick said. "And we're trying to create consistency for our players."

Redick called Reaves "Mr. Consistency" earlier this season. He had 13 points in the third quarter, including five straight on difficult fadeway jumpers that pushed the lead to 17 with 6:41 to go in the third. After his step-back three from the wing kissed off the glass and rattled through the rim, Reaves shrugged.

"It was 100% lucky," Reaves said. "Honestly, when I stepped back and shot it, I was like, 'this is some bull— Luka would shoot.'"

Reaves shot Doncic's shots and then talked Doncic's talk. After hitting the dagger shot to put the Lakers up by eight with 44.5 seconds left, Reaves turned and waved goodbye directly to the two fans sitting courtside.

#2921. Will this week's blue wave election usher in a Democratic president in 2028?

In surfer slang, a wave that lasts a long time is a "runner." A key question for investors is whether the blue wave that washed over the country Tuesday was that kind of wave, and could it ferry Democrats to victory in 2028.

Statistically, the answer is it should have little effect.

CNBC analyzed elections since 1938 and looked at the biggest midterm wave elections where the party in the White House lost seats to determine whether they portend anything about the presidential election two years later.

Tuesday's election was the biggest House seat loss for the incumbent party in the White House since 2018, but it only barely makes the historical Top 10. It ranks 10th in CNBC's analysis using an estimate of 41 House seats gained by the opposition party as close races are still being tallied. The largest loss came in 1938 when Democrats lost 72 House seats amid a renewed recession, opposition to court-packing by President Franklin Delano Roosevelt and the president's battle with conservatives in his own party. Roosevelt went on to win reelection two years later in 1940.

In more modern times, Republicans swept to power in the House in 2010 two years into Barack Obama's first presidential term, with Democrats losing 64 seats, though retaining control of the Senate. Obama went on to reelection two years later. Of the top four wave elections, which all occurred before a president's final term, the incumbent went on to win reelection each time.

But for the next five waves, the incumbent party lost, including John F. Kennedy beating Richard Nixon in 1960 after Republicans lost seats under President Dwight Eisenhower in 1958; and Joe Biden beating Donald Trump in 2020 after Republicans lost seats in 2018.

Jay Campbell, partner at Hart Associates, the Democratic pollster for the CNBC All-America Economic Survey, said the reason midterm waves may not carry forward is the importance of individual characters in presidential elections.

"A lot can happen in two years. And while presidential elections are certainly subject to the overall political environment, they are also very much about each party's nominee," Campbell said. "If candidate quality matters in congressional elections, it matters 1,000 times more in presidential ones."

It's a long way to 2028 and this wave, gnarly as it may have been, could statistically peter out just as easily as it could wash Democrats back into the White House.
