#2877. Weekend trading of Nvidia scandal shines light on new financial products

As news of a potential scandal surrounding Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang began to trickle out on Sunday, investors faced a simple question: what could it mean for the stock?

One answer could be found in an obscure but growing corner of the markets: perpetual futures.

On Sunday at 3 p.m. ET, well before pre-market equity trading had started, a number of traders began placing bearish bets on Nvidia perpetual futures. Specifically, perpetuals on Nvidia traded first, with Hyperliquid's xyz contract slipping to as low as $180.15 on $200 million of volume by 3:30 p.m. E.T. Both equity shares and perps on Nvidia fell below $178 overnight. It traded 7% lower on Monday.

Sunday's trading shines a light on a product that is increasingly filling a market void when traditional markets are off-line.

In their simplest form, perpetual futures are highly leveraged bets that never expire. They can track almost any asset class and trade around the clock, seven days a week.

"Perps have filled a significant gap or void in the market," said Rich Repetto, a former exchange analyst and market structure expert.  "They provide access as well as price discovery to markets when there otherwise would be a vacuum."

When Iran launched a drone attack against Israel on Saturday, April 13 in 2024, traditional markets were completely closed, and crypto native exchanges that offered perpetual futures were the only global proxy for real time market sentiment in oil and the S&P 500. As that conflict escalated, perpetuals — or perps as they are called — became a go to for many traders looking for inputs over the weekend.

"It should be very clear at this point that news doesn't stop when the market is closed," said David Schamis, CEO of Hyperliquid Strategies (PURR), the publicly-traded treasury company investing in HYPE tokens that power the de-entralized exchange. " Investors want deep liquidity and price discovery when it is needed, not just nine-to-five."

Perps weren't the only new, exotic product to see increased volume over the weekend. Prediction contracts tied to Huang's potential exit also saw a spike in activity. Specifically, a Kalshi contract asking when Jensen Huang might leave as Nvidia CEO, which was listed just days before the bombshell reports, saw over $105,000 traded on Sunday.

And pre-market trading in options on Nvidia – which began trading just weeks ago – saw $15 million in options premium traded in Nvidia across about 40,000 contracts. A little over half the contracts were puts, with just under $10 million of premium tied to puts, SpotGamma data show.

The emergence of new products highlight the growing need for more information as traditional financial exchanges extend market hours trading.

"When the markets close, we lose real-time feedback," said Options Action contributor, Mike Khouw. "Futures, prediction markets and expanded afterhours trading helps fill that gap."

Disclosure: CNBC and Kalshi have a commercial relationship that includes customer acquisition and a minority investment.

#2876. Savannah Arts, Richmond Hill and Islands girls impress at state cross-country

The high school cross-country season came to an end on the rugged course in Carrollton as teams closed out their seasons on Friday and Saturday, Nov. 6th and 7th.

Several teams from the Greater Savannah area had big-time showings in the meets.

The Savannah Arts girls won their third straight Class 2A title and their fourth in the last five seasons, while the Panther boys team had its best ever finish earning a podium spot at third place.

The Richmond Hill boys and girls had the best individual and team times as both squads reached the podium with the girls taking third and the boys placing fourth in the Class 7A meet.

In Class 3A, the Islands girls had their best ever showing in the state meet with a second place finish.

The Savannah Arts girls put a bow on an incredible season in which the team won all 10 of its races, with the six scoring Panther runners all finishing in the Top 25 on Saturday.

Senior Francie Tedder was second individually with a time of 20:38.79 on the 3.1 mile course. Morgan Irvine was eighth in 21:30.36, one spot ahead of McKay Bennett (21:40.35). Ave Treece was 12th in 21:55.74, Madeline Balthrop took 17th in 22:33.28 and Eleanora Smith was 24th in 22:58.05. Freshman Lorena Nell was 31st (23:29.39).

The Panther boys took third led by sophomore Logan Brodhead who finished eighth (17:57.60), Nico Klaras, who was 13th in 18:09.16, Walter Yang who was 15th in 18:14.67 and William McCarn, who finished 22nd in 18:28.94.

Tedder closed out a memorable senior campaign where she won five races and finished second in her other three. She said Panther coach Steve Ricciardi's impact on the program was immeasurable as he helped them prepare for the rugged, hilly Carrollton course.

"There aren't may hills in Savannah and Coach Ricciardi got out on the 52nd street bridge over Truman Parkway and used his weed whacker to help make a trail up the hill for us there," Tedder said. "That helped us get ready for 'Separation Slope' on the state course. It was so exciting to win our third straight state meet and see all of our hard work pay off. Coach Ricciardi got us ready and we had four runners who were on all three of those teams, so we had that experience and were ready to win again."

Richmond Hill also had a stellar performance on Saturday. The girls were third behind junior Chloe Miller, who posted the best time of any Savannah area female runner with a sixth place finish in 19:21.42. Gabriella Francis took ninth (19:33.75) and Julia Anastasio finished 15th in 20:08.27. Freshman Koeyn Harris was 22nd in 20:20.04 and her classmate, Lucy Puskar was 29th in 20:40.06 with Lena Tocco finishing 47th in 21:25.77.

The Wildcat boys reached the podium with a fourth place finish. Liam Penttila closed out a special freshman season with a fourth place finish in 16:02.67, the best time of any male runner in the Savannah area. Derek Feske was 14th in 16:44.36. Eddie Howell finished 19th (16:53.95) and Caden Gross finished 63rd in 17:45.16. Benjamin Tocco ran a time of 18:05.10 and finished 87th. Lealond Hodges ran a time of 18:20.99.

The Islands girls team made school history with a second place finish in Class 3A. Sophomore Leila Kellison led the way for the Sharks by placing fourth with a time of 22:17.60. Zeycan Agbas (24:10.99) finished 20th and Bella LaPlante was 35th in 25:27.77. Kaleigh Toler ran a time of 25:45.55 to place 40th.

In the Class 5A boys race, Benedictine's Cole Minnich had an excellent race as the senior finished fifth with a time of 17:04.70. Minnich battled back from an injury this season and the state race was just his fourth event of the year.

#2875. Jaguars Add Safety Jessie Bates in Trade with Falcons

With the Jacksonville Jaguars recently rumored to be in the 2026 trade market for a big fish, the team made its first big splash, completing another deal with the Atlanta Falcons. This one brings in a legitimate difference-maker in the secondary. According to NFL Insider Adam Schefter, Jacksonville is acquiring safety Jessie Bates from the Falcons in exchange for a 2027 second-round pick and a 2028 fifth-round pick.

It's the latest move in a trade pipeline that has been building between the two franchises since James Gladstone became general manager.

Bates is a player we initially put on the radar about three weeks ago in our defensive trade targets piece. We projected his trade value at a conditional 2027 third-round pick that could rise to a second, so the final price landed right around where we expected. Bates is in the final year of his contract and is expected to want, and likely get, a new deal. Atlanta evidently wasn't eager to pay it, which opened the door for Jacksonville.

Per Jonathan Jones and Ian Rapoport, Atlanta will pay most of Bates' 2026 $6.5M salary to complete the deal.

Bates arrives with a résumé built on production and takeaways:

3× Second-team All-Pro (2020, 2023, 2025)

14 interceptions coming over the last three and a half seasons in Atlanta

2026 PFF overall grade of 84.1 (fourth best)

2026 PFF coverage grade of 87.9 (second best)

Most importantly, against the backdrop of the Eric Murray injury that took the Jacksonville safety for the season, durability matters. Bates hasn't missed a game since signing with Atlanta, starting 60 of 60 games.

With Jacksonville recently losing Murray for the year, that left the safety room needing a boost. While Ray Lane has played well during Murray's absence, rather than patching the spot, the front office went after one of the better playmakers at the position. This move still allows Lane to play the third safety looks that Campanile loves, while pairing Bates with chess piece Antonio Johnson on base downs.

For me, the biggest takeaway is what Bates does for the scheme. He's the first true rangy, sideline-to-sideline safety the Jaguars have had under defensive coordinator Anthony Campanile. Over the past two seasons, Jacksonville has leaned heavily on two-high safety looks. Bates gives them a ballhawk who can play over the top, which opens up more Cover 1 and Cover 3 looks, with Bates playing as the security blanket over top. Campanile's defense already thrives on takeaways, disguise, and variety. With Bates in the mix, it should be even more unpredictable and even more dangerous to opposing quarterbacks. Through week 8, Bates has Pro Football Focus's third-highest safety grade when playing man coverage, coupled with the 11th-highest grade in zone coverage.

What are your initial thoughts on the trade, Duval? Is that a big enough fish for your appetite? Or is the cost too high? Drop your thoughts in the comments!

#2874. Nets routed by Mavericks in sluggish performance

Dallas Mavericks forward Cooper Flagg (32) reacts after a dunk during a game against the Brooklyn Nets in Dallas, Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

And then a team like this, when they're in rhythm with the players that they have, it's when you can get hurt," head coach Jordi Fernández said. "They made some tough ones, and we also allowed some. I thought offensively, we created good shots, but we again turned it over too much — and the nature of the turnovers, 17 turnovers for 24 points. That's just not acceptable.

"And a lot of them were like we're trying to run in transition and we give it to the other team, and those are tough. We still created 21 turnovers with 25 points, and I think that that's the pressure and who we want to be. But overall, a poor defensive game."

The Nets (2-8) saw Cooper Flagg dominate with 25 points, 12 assists and five rebounds while Kyrie Irving scored 21 against his former team.

Michael Porter Jr. led the Nets with 19 points and nine boards, but Julius Randle struggled with just 13 and finished a minus-33. This team isn't capable of winning with that sort of performance from Randle.

The Nets allowed 57.1 percent shooting, including 61.9 percent in a first half that saw them trail 66-46, and unlike the Hawks a day earlier, they never rallied.

Dallas Mavericks' Kyrie Irving (11) drives to the basket against the Brooklyn Nets on Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026, at American Airlines Center in Dallas. Getty Images

The Nets had caught Atlanta on the tail end of a back-to-back and built a 17-point cushion. They even led by four with 1:45 to play before coughing up 10 unanswered points, letting the Hawks close the game on a 14-7 run.

Fernández had hoped they could replicate their energy from the night before, but actually sustain it. Instead, apparently they had exhausted it.

"The second quarter, we didn't make shots, didn't play defense," Randle said. "They were giving a lot of stuff off of switching. But credit to them, they executed really well."

The Nets shot 38.8 percent and 11-for-39 from deep.

Brooklyn Nets forward Michael Porter Jr., center, tries to score against Dallas Mavericks defenders Daniel Gafford, left, and Cooper Flagg on Nov. 8, 2026. AP Photo/LM Otero

Brooklyn fell behind by 13 in the first quarter, and that deficit doubled to 26 in the even sloppier second.

When Randle threw the ball behind Porter coming upcourt, it was a turnover on the sideline with 1:48 left in the half. The latter banged the ball on his head in frustration.

But the sequence that really typified the evening for Brooklyn?

Noah Clowney got whistled for a foul on Morez Johnson Jr., and the two had to be separated with 3:51 left in the third quarter as the officials went to replay to see whether the Net forward had committed a flagrant. The replay showed that Johnson had actually brazenly knocked Clowney to the court on the other end of the floor moments earlier, so he was assessed a technical.

Then Moritz Wagner clanked the ensuing free throw with the Nets trailing 92-62.

Clowney promptly fouled out to salt the wound.

"We couldn't make shots, they made a lot of them. And then once adversity hits, it seems to be really difficult for us to kind of get our head out of … whatever it is," Wagner admitted.

"It's never all bad, and it's not all good, you know. It never is as bad as it looks, but our body language does drop. We lack a little maturity there as a group. It's nobody's fault; it's just everybody kind of in new roles, everybody getting used to it, which is an excuse. It's something we have to look in the mirror for, everybody for himself and kind of figure it out."

#2873. Congress returns with government funding, Pete Hegseth and the debt limit on the agenda

The House and Senate are due to return to Washington on Monday with a crowded legislative agenda in the final weeks before the end of the 119th Congress.

Congress will need to act quickly to extend government funding, which expires Dec. 11. Lawmakers will need to pass the National Defense Authorization Act, and they may tackle ambitious proposals including a construction permitting package and a bill to regulate name, image and likeness rules in college sports.

The work will commence under a new dynamic. Republicans come back to the Capitol diminished after suffering substantial losses in this week's midterms. Democrats swept to a commanding majority in the House with at least 255 seats in the 435-member chamber and could emerge with as many as 56 seats in the Senate.

With majorities in both chambers looming, Democrats will have little incentive to cut deals with their GOP counterparts.

The most pressing concern lawmakers will face when they return is funding the government. Current funding expires Dec. 11. Congress must pass another continuing resolution to retain current spending levels or a full-year suite of appropriations bills before then to avoid a government shutdown.

Appropriations bills and continuing resolutions require bipartisan majorities due to the Senate's 60-vote filibuster threshold. Democrats are unlikely to negotiate a full year of government funding before the new Congress is sworn in, given that they will soon hold commanding majorities in both chambers and can use that to leverage concessions from the White House.

"This former preschool teacher is going to teach President Trump a simple lesson: The power of the purse belongs to CONGRESS!" Sen. Patty Murray, D-Wash., the presumptive chair of the Appropriations Committee, said in an X post Tuesday as election results rolled in. "It's NOT Trump's money. Taxpayer dollars belong to the American PEOPLE."

Adding another wrinkle to the funding push is the electoral loss of Sen. Susan Collins, R-Maine, who chairs the Senate Appropriations Committee and is one of the "four corners" of spending talks. Collins lost reelection to Democrat Troy Jackson, ending a five-term career in the Senate.

Some lawmakers, including Sen. Kevin Cramer, R-N.D., have called for Congress to raise the debt limit before Republicans lose control.

In September, the federal debt hit $40 trillion for the first time, bumping up against the $41.1 trillion ceiling written into the GOP's 2025 budget reconciliation law known as the "one big beautiful bill."

The Bipartisan Policy Center, a nonprofit that tracks the debt limit, said Treasury is likely to hit its maximum borrowing capacity by mid-summer. The U.S. may no longer be able to meet all its obligations by November 2027, according to a Bank of America estimate from Nov. 6.

But Democrats may instead prefer to use the debt limit as a bargaining chip in the next Congress.

Republicans could theoretically raise the debt ceiling in a budget reconciliation package, as they did with their 2025 law. But cobbling together a post-election coalition to clear another reconciliation bill with exceptionally narrow margins would be difficult. Budget reconciliation, which allows party-line bills to skirt the filibuster in the Senate, requires near-total unity.

Congressional leadership, particularly on the Republican side, is likely to have difficulty with lawmaker attendance after dozens of members lost their seats in an electoral bloodbath. Leadership will also struggle to whip members into line, especially those who do not have another election to run.

Top senators on energy committees struck a deal on a permitting bill before the election. That accord to make construction easier could now be in jeopardy, though the Senate plans to hold a procedural vote to advance it Monday evening, according to a Senate aide familiar with the schedule. The person asked not to be named because the move has not been announced.

The resounding majorities Democrats won in both the House and Senate may give them incentive to torpedo the permitting deal in hopes of securing a better one later. The deal also may have been in trouble prior to the election, with top Democrats in the House not supporting the version their counterparts in the Senate accepted.

Politico reported Thursday that Democratic Minority Leader and likely next House Speaker Hakeem Jeffries, D-N.Y., did not support the permitting deal.

Lawmakers have long sought, and failed to reach, a bipartisan, bicameral deal on permitting reform. Democrats want to speed up the buildout of clean energy. Republicans have long complained that the federal permitting process and environmental laws that comprise it stymie the U.S.'s ability to build critical infrastructure.

The House passed a version of the NDAA, which would authorize more than $1 trillion in defense spending, in July. But Senate Democrats blocked their chamber's version of the bill that same month over concerns about the Iran war.

"Donald Trump is dragging America deeper into a war in Iran with no authorization, no plan, and no exit strategy. Democrats will not go along," Senate Minority Leader Chuck Schumer, D-N.Y., said in a statement at the time.

The NDAA is typically seen as a must-pass, bipartisan bill.

The legislation as it stood in July also drew pushback from businesses over a provision that would restrict Pentagon contractors from executing stock buybacks and paying dividends unless they have the Defense Department's approval.

A new wrinkle threatens to derail the NDAA process during the lame duck, however. Defense Secretary Pete Hegseth faces a vote on articles of impeachment upon Congress' return, and Democrats have already been highly skeptical of the Pentagon's asks in this year's budget as the expensive Iran war drags on.

Other priorities could also get floor time.

The Senate in September approved, in a bipartisan vote, a package that would establish federal rules for the ways in which college athletes are compensated. But that legislation has many opponents in the House, particularly among Democrats in the chamber, making its passage more complicated.

There's also been a yearslong effort to ban members of Congress from owning or trading individual stocks.

In previous Congresses, the effort was stymied by leadership from both parties. And more recently, a bipartisan push to pass legislation was derailed after House Democrats demanded the ban also apply to the president.

One GOP-led proposal, called the Stop Insider Trading Act, passed the House with some bipartisan support in July, but met with Democratic opposition because it would apply only to Congress and would not require lawmakers to divest existing holdings.

Sen. Todd Young, R-Ind., called for action on the issue in an X post Thursday. Young is a co-sponsor of the Senate version of the Stop Insider Trading Act.

"We have a great opportunity in the lame duck session to restore a small amount of trust by banning Members of Congress from trading stocks. I've been talking about this for many months, and it should be a no-brainer," Young said.

Democrats, however, are eager to claim a victory on congressional stock trading in the next Congress.

Jeffries, in a letter to fellow House members announcing his candidacy for speaker, listed it as a priority as part of Democrats' anti-corruption agenda.

"We must root out corrupt price gouging, clean up the mess at the Supreme Court and ban Members of Congress from trading stocks. Accountability begins at home," Jeffries wrote.

#2872. Home listings pile up in October, but sellers won't slash prices

Homebuyers continue to retreat, but sellers are still digging in, refusing to cut prices or give up. As a result, September's frozen housing market is now October's frozen and increasingly bloated market.

Active inventory in October hit 183,500 homes, up 7.4% year-over-year, according to Realtor.com. That is the fastest annual growth in eight months. Active listings have been rising each month since February. This is despite the fact that new listings are actually falling, down 7.5% from October 2025.

The market has seen several years of extremely lean supply, driven by the homebuying frenzy during the first two years of the pandemic, when mortgage rates hit more than a dozen record lows. Now supply nationally is just 6.6% below typical pre-pandemic levels, the narrowest gap since the pandemic.

"The market is cooling on both sides, but in different ways. Buyers are stepping back as higher mortgage rates cut into their buying power, while sellers are listing less often without a broad increase in delistings or price cuts," said Danielle Hale, chief economist at Realtor.com. "We're seeing a pause in new supply, but owners who already have homes on the market appear to be largely unphased."

Just over 20% of sellers cut prices in October, about the same as both September and October of last year. There was also no meaningful increase in delistings by sellers.

Mortgage rates came down a little bit in October month-to-month, but they are still over a full percentage point higher than a year ago. That continued to weigh on sales. Newly pending sales, representing signed contracts, not closings, dropped 16.8% in October year-over-year, according to Zillow. That was the biggest annual drop since August 2023 and slowest October since 2018.

While rates fell slightly, home prices continued to gain. Zillow estimated the monthly mortgage payment on the typical U.S. home was $2,004 in October, assuming a 20% down payment and excluding taxes and insurance. That is an increase of 12.8% from last year and 4.8% higher than in September, the biggest one-month jump in four years.

"November is shaping up to have an even larger contraction, but the silver lining is that next month is likely going to be the worst of it," said Mischa Fisher, Zillow's chief economist, in a release. "I expect the pace of the slowdown to ease into the new year. There are deals to be had for buyers able to brave the affordability headwinds in the coming months, and the continued inventory buildup means there is a lot of upside for sales into next year if we get rate relief."

#2871. Yankees linked to blockbuster trade for former World Series champion

The New York Yankees have been connected to a blockbuster MLB trade idea involving Texas Rangers shortstop Corey Seager as they look to address a glaring hole at third base.

The proposal is considered a long shot, but it includes an interesting twist that could make the deal more appealing: keeping Seager at shortstop while top prospect George Lombard Jr. moves to third base.

MORE: Predicting MLB free agent landing spots in 2026-27 offseason

Apr 6, 2026; Arlington, Texas, USA; Texas Rangers shortstop Corey Seager (5) bats during the game between the Rangers and the Mariners at Globe Life Field. Mandatory Credit: Jerome Miron-Imagn Images

The idea was explored by the New York Post as the Yankees begin looking for solutions at third base following a disappointing season from Ryan McMahon. Seager would represent a major offensive upgrade if he can stay healthy, and his postseason experience would bring another proven winner to the New York lineup.

The 32-year-old is a two-time World Series champion and two-time World Series MVP. He helped lead the Rangers to their first championship in 2023 and has established himself as one of baseball's premier offensive shortstops when healthy.

However, there are legitimate reasons why this would be a risky move. Seager finished the 2026 season with a career-worst 106 OPS+ while appearing in just 102 games. He also recently underwent elbow surgery, adding another layer of uncertainty to a potential deal.

The financial commitment would be substantial, too. Seager has approximately $165 million remaining on his contract through 2031. Texas could potentially be motivated to move the contract as it looks to manage its payroll, but the Yankees would have to be comfortable taking on a significant financial obligation.

Aug 28, 2026; Bronx, New York, USA; New York Yankees shortstop George Lombard Jr. (96) reacts after an out against the Boston Red Sox during the fifth inning at Yankee Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Tom Horak-Imagn Images Credit: IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect (Reuters)

The most interesting part of the proposed trade involves how the Yankees would handle their infield afterward. Rather than immediately moving Seager to third base, the suggestion is to let him remain at shortstop for the 2027 season while Lombard takes over at the hot corner.

Lombard made his MLB debut in August and is viewed as an important part of New York's future. He has experience at multiple infield positions in the minor leagues, giving the Yankees some flexibility if they decide to pursue Seager.

The arrangement would allow New York to add an established star without immediately forcing Seager into a new defensive position. The Yankees could then reassess the infield alignment down the road as Lombard continues developing.

Of course, getting a deal done would be difficult. Seager has a full no-trade clause, meaning he would have to approve a move to New York. His contract and recent injury concerns also make this a significant gamble.

Still, the Yankees have a clear need at third base and could use another impact bat. If Texas is willing to make the financial commitment manageable and Seager approves the destination, this long-shot trade could give New York one of the biggest additions of the offseason.

#2870. Eagles-Giants snap counts

The official snap counts from the Philadelphia Eagles' Week 9 loss to the New York Giants are in. Let's take a look and run through some analysis.

Jordan Mailata is the only player who's played 100% of the Eagles' offensive snaps this season. He's also played 11.16% of the Eagles' special teams snaps.

The Eagles' starting offensive line configuration in each of their last four games has played 100% of the team's snaps on offense.

Darius Cooper and Makai Lemon continue to be the Eagles' top two receivers with both DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks missing time.

Saquon Barkley played his highest number of snaps in a game this season. It was his third-highest snap count percentage. Barkley's 13 carries resulted in just 22 yards. Again, it's admirable that he wants to play through injuries. But it doesn't seem wise for the team to keep putting out there at far less than 100%.

This should've been Hollywood Brown's last game as an Eagle. Trade him for literally anything you can get in return.

Elijah Mitchell had some of the lowest of football IQ plays you'll ever see on Sunday. He was wide open just short of the marker and he failed to get a first down. Then he stayed in bounds instead of getting to the sideline late in the game with the Eagles having zero timeouts. Mitchell is out of practice squad elevations. There's no need to keep him on the PS anymore and he clearly doesn't deserve a spot on the roster. Time to move on.

Facing 2nd-and-7 at the plus 47-yard line while trailing by eight points in the fourth quarter, the Eagles decided it was a good time to get Elijah Moore a designed touch on a screen play. It resulted in a three-yard loss. Why was that the time to get him involved? And in that capacity? Nonsensical. Moore is now down to four receptions for 17 yards this season.

For the second week in a row, Johnny Mundt played his lowest snap count percentage of the season. Seems like the Eagles are phasing him out, as they should. Less Mundt and Grant Calcaterra, more Eli Stowers.

Fred Johnson saw some playing time as a sixth offensive lineman. The Eagles scored their sole touchdown on one of these plays.

Willie Lampkin saw some playing time as a fullback.

Riq Woolen missed three snaps after getting banged up in the first half.

Jeremiah Trotter Jr. led the Eagles in tackles once again with 11 total.

Nolan Smith is quietly having a nice season in a lost year for the team; he has five sacks in eight games played.

Marcus Epps was back as the Eagles' safety next to Andrew Mukuba in nickel packages, replacing Kenny Moore II in that role.

Jordan Davis logged two TFLs against the Giants, giving him two on the season. Not great that it took until Week 9 for him to show up in that category.

Jonathan Greenard might be getting healthier? He logged his highest snap count percentage of the season on Sunday. He logged one sack, one TFL, and tied with Zack Baun for a team-high two quarterback hits. Pro Football Focus charted Greenard with a team-high five pressures.

Baun suffered a back injury in the third quarter and did not return. Despite this, he led the Eagles with a team-high seven stops (tackles constituted as a failure for the offense). He came to play against the Giants.

Jalyx Hunt continues to be on a pitch count as he plays through injuries.

With Jonathan Jones out, Kelee Ringo was the top backup corner and briefly saw snaps when Woolen missed time.

Cole Wisniewski exclusively played on special teams after being temporarily elevated from the practice squad. Would be good to see the rookie worked into the defense at some point.

Braden Mann had a wild day with an 86-yard punt and a -2 yard punt in the same game.

Second week in a row where A.J. Epenesa was limited to special teams duty.

Jaydon Blue handled kick return duty. The Eagles really don't want to use him on offense.

ACTIVE: Tanner McKee, Cole Payton, Jake Majors

INACTIVE: DeVonta Smith, Jihaad Campbell, Dontayvion Wicks, Jonathan Jones, Michael Carter II, Lane Johnson, Britain Covey

McKee was reportedly the No. 2 QB behind Joe Milton despite the fact he was ruled questionable to play with a rib injury.

Payton was active instead of being the emergency third quarterback because the Eagles filled up their inactive spots with injured players.

At least some of the Eagles' injured players could be back on the field after the team's Week 10 bye.

As a reminder, Johnson is set to remain on the Eagles' roster until June 2027, even though he retired.

#2869. Rapid Recap: Celtics 117, Bucks 97

The Milwaukee Bucks started strong but faded in a 117-97 loss to the Boston Celtics. In a rematch of Wednesday's back-and-forth loss to the Celtics, the Bucks led by as much as 14 in the first quarter, but ceded ground thanks to Boston's strong shooting and pesky defense. The Celtics retook the lead at the start of the second quarter and never looked back in a comfortable 20-point victory. Kyle Kuzma led Milwaukee with 27 points on 10/17 shooting, followed by Brayden Burries (13 points) and Tyler Herro (13 points). Paul George scored 27 on 18 shots, while Jayson Tatum (21), Neemias Queta (13), Payton Pritchard (12), and Mitchell Robinson (12) all logged double-digit points.

Milwaukee rolled out its new starting lineup of Brayden Burries, Tyler Herro, Jaime Jaquez Jr., Kyle Kuzma and Myles Turner. Boston started Mike Conley, Derrick White, Paul George, Jayson Tatum and Neemias Queta. The story early was Kyle Kuzma, who dropped 16 points on 6/10 shooting in the first quarter alone. The Bucks led 20-6 after five minutes of play, forcing the Celtics to call a timeout as Kuzma had already dropped 14 of those 16—he was hot. But of course, the Celtics remained calm and worked their way back into the game, largely thanks to Tatum's high-level shooting and aggressive defence that forced turnovers and let Boston score easy points. Tatum finished the quarter with 16 points on 4/9 shooting as Boston strung together a 10-0 run to end the period, with the Bucks leading by just two, 27-25.

Paul George connected on a corner three to give the Celtics their first lead just seconds into the second quarter. Boston kept clicking from the perimeter and pestered Milwaukee's ball handlers. The Bucks really hit an offensive wall at this point, connecting on just two threes from 12 attempts. In comparison, Boston looked at ease; White got to his spots at the midrange, Queta consistently beat Turner on the interior, and George splashed three after three. In fact, PG finished with a ridiculous 16 points in the second quarter on 6/9 shooting, helping Boston outscore Milwaukee 33-18 in the period. Tatum found Queta wide open in the paint, who threw down a big jam to put the Celtics up 58-45 at halftime.

Things got uglier for the Bucks in the third, as the Celtics opened with a 7-0 run and stretched their lead to 20. Herro also hobbled off the court with an apparent knee injury after being fouled by Derrick White, but thankfully, returned later in the game. To their credit, the Bucks showed resolve; Turner converted an and-one layup over Queta and Kuzma knocked down a three to trigger a Boston timeout. Ousmane Dieng saw his first minutes in nearly four games and made his cameo count with a pull-up midrange jumper to cut the Celtics' lead to 16. Ryan Rollins also began to pick up steam from long range, but Boston still held a huge advantage, leading 91-74 entering the final frame.

Pritchard fired a step-back three, and Brayden Burries responded with a midrange jumper to start the fourth quarter. Boston continued to dominate Milwaukee, though they slowed marginally as they brought in their deep rotation to finish the game. That was no matter, though, because they still had Pritchard on the court, who connected on three three-pointers within two minutes. Special hat tip to Dieng, though, who scored eight points in the fourth quarter along to get his year heading back in the right direction. The Celtics coasted to a comfortable 20-point lead, dropping the Bucks to 1-9 in their first 10 games.

The battle on the glass was one-sided today. Boston grabbed 20 offensive boards to Milwaukee's nine and led the game in rebounds, 48-36. Boston picked up a lot of second-chance points through the efforts of Queta and Robinson, which ultimately helped the Celtics pull away in the second quarter.

Ousmane Dieng deserves a permanent spot in the rotation after that fourth quarter. Dieng shined in his limited minutes, scoring 10 points on perfect 3/3 shooting. Dieng has struggled this season, especially finding a rhythm from beyond the arc and competing with wings for playing time, but he certainly took a step forward against Boston. It could translate to more meaningful minutes in Tuesday's outing against the New York Knicks.

#2868. Trump questions midterm election results, revives voting-machine conspiracy

President Donald Trump on Monday questioned the legitimacy of the midterm election results, resurrecting his pattern of raising doubts about electoral losses for himself and other Republicans.

Trump's social media post raising questions about the election came six days after his fellow Republicans suffered historic defeats at the polls nationwide, including in several GOP strongholds like Texas and South Carolina.

"Does anybody really believe that this 'Election' was honest," Trump wrote on Truth Social.

"How could the Polls be so wrong??? Some early voting 'dumps' were so large that it was impossible to win, no matter how many people went to vote," the president said. "It's all about the machines, which are far more important than the voter!"

After his electoral loss to former President Joe Biden in 2020, Trump argued, without evidence, that he only lost as a result of widespread ballot fraud.

Trump's claims, which were joined by dozens of unsuccessful court cases challenging election procedures, culminated in the Jan. 6, 2021, attack on the U.S. Capitol by a mob of his supporters after he spoke at a rally outside the White House.

The riot disrupted a joint session of Congress for hours. Congress later that day confirmed Biden's election by the Electoral College.

#2867. Nvidia's CEO turmoil gives newly empowered Democrats fresh ammunition in pursuit of oversight

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's close ties to the U.S. government were already poised to loosen with Democrats winning control of Congress and promising far more oversight of AI development than what the chip giant has experienced under Republican leadership.

That was before Huang admitted on Sunday to having an extramarital affair with a businesswoman who intelligence agencies say is a Chinese spy. The woman, Alice Huang, is affiliated with an Nvidia customer called Megaspeed that's been accused of helping restricted Chinese companies acquire Nvidia's artificial intelligence chips.

Huang apologized to staffers in a note on Sunday for being "unfaithful to my wife," and said the company's board was investigating any related national security concerns. Nvidia's board said it has "confidence in Jensen," who co-founded the company in 1993 and has led it ever since, building a net worth of more than $150 billion in the process.

Even before last week's midterm elections, which saw Democrats win sweeping majorities in the House and Senate, vocal party leaders wanted answers from Huang about China's access to advanced Nvidia chips and his company's lobbying for favorable legislation. Sen. Elizabeth Warren of Massachusetts said days before the election, when the spy story was first surfacing, that "we must pass bipartisan legislation to keep our advanced AI technology out of China's hands." Months before that, in June, Huang declined an invitation from Warren to testify before the Senate Banking Committee.

With Democrats about to take the gavel, Delaware Sen. Chris Coons, who is in line to chair the Senate Foreign Relations Committee, wrote on X on Saturday that "Congress will investigate and get answers," following the allegations that appeared in a New York Times report Friday.

It's a sharp reversal for Huang, who gained first-name status with President Donald Trump and has been closely allied with the administration in trying to limit AI regulation. Nvidia shares plunged 12% in the two days after the election, pacing a drop in AI-related stocks, on fears that incoming lawmakers, at the federal and state levels, and a host of newly passed ballot measures will slow the buildout and halt the AI boom.

Huang is a singular voice in the AI industry, as his company's technology is used to train nearly all major AI models. He's also turned Nvidia into an essential source of financing for AI startups, and pulled together some of the biggest names on Wall Street into a group that announced plans in August to raise $500 billion for investing in the buildout.

At Nvidia, Huang is the sole public face of the company and has no clear second-in-command. He decides which projects are a strategic priority, and frequently sends short emails to individual engineers about details. Huang has said he has 60 direct reports and reviews every employee's salary. Meanwhile, he packs his calendar with meetings with world leaders and CEOs, alongside keynote speeches at conferences and trade shows.

"Nvidia is not an ordinary company and Jensen is not an ordinary CEO; he IS Nvidia," Bernstein analyst Stacy Rasgon wrote in a note on Sunday, adding that it's not clear who would succeed him.

Patrick Moorhead, CEO at Moor Insights & Strategy, said in an email that "Jensen holding so much power is why Nvidia moves faster than anyone."

Unlike many of his tech peers, Huang is not chairman of the board. Rather, he's one of 11 board members, and there's no designated chair.

One investor close to Huang told CNBC that, depending on what the board's investigation finds, he could foresee Huang taking a leave of absence.

"Today's news will not slow Nvidia," a company spokesperson said in a statement. "Jensen is committed to continuing to lead Nvidia as CEO without interruption."

But scrutiny of Nvidia is set to intensify on Wall Street and in Washington, where Huang's level of influence is almost certain to change.

He has been a major advocate for more chip exports to China, arguing that it's better for AI labs there to build on American technology than to encourage the development of domestic alternatives. Huang joined Trump on Air Force One in May on a China trip that's now raised additional questions about what the administration and Huang knew about Chinese spying at the time.

One question Democrats are likely to probe is whether the Trump administration was aware of Huang's relationship with the alleged Chinese spy, and whether the Nvidia CEO influenced American policy, said Sam Bresnick, a research fellow at the Center for Security and Emerging Technology.

"That is going to be very high up on the Democrats' lists of people to investigate to try to figure out to what extent was the Trump administration aware of what was going on," Bresnick said.

But pressure isn't just coming from Democrats. House Republican leaders have already asked Huang for a closed-door briefing on Chinese spying issues later this month.

Trump has continued to back Huang, telling reporters on Sunday that he's an "honorable guy" and has "done an amazing job for our country."

"I think he probably has an excuse for it," Trump said of the incident.

Amid all the noise about the election results and the spying revelations, Nvidia is facing a crucial stretch on the markets.

Neoclouds CoreWeave and Nebius, which are both backed by Nvidia and are big buyers of its chips, are scheduled to report earnings this week after their stocks lost roughly half their value over the past month. Investors are particularly concerned about their hefty debt loads and how they'll continue to fund their buildouts with interest rates rising and lenders turning skittish.

Nvidia has its own earnings report coming on Nov. 17, when Huang, assuming he sticks with the typical format, will face questions from analysts. And then there's the Anthropic initial public offering, which is critical for Nvidia as it's both an investor in the Claude creator and counts the AI lab as a significant customer.

A couple of weeks after earnings, Nvidia is hosting a large conference in Washington, D.C., where Huang is scheduled to give the keynote on Dec. 1.

Bullish investors and analysts can still point to the insatiable demand for Nvidia's technology. The company recently projected 70% sales growth in its upcoming fiscal year. Analysts polled by FactSet expect nearly $694 billion in fiscal 2028 sales.

"We believe demand for the company's products seems unlikely to be affected," Rasgon wrote. "But it seems plausible to postulate increased risks (regulatory etc.) as AI buildouts become an increasingly political issue."

— CNBC's Seema Mody, Katie Tarasov, Ashley Capoot and MacKenzie Sigalos contributed reporting to this report.

#2866. The AI ‘credit card’ is getting costlier as CoreWeave bond yield tops 16%

Debt markets are sending a message to AI data center builders: we're hiking the interest rate on your credit card and may put some new curbs on your credit limit.

That analogy comes courtesy of Jefferies global head of equity strategy, Christopher Wood, who put it in a note on Friday: "The most plausible way the AI capex arms race will end is that the credit card will be taken away."

That isn't happening yet, but credit markets have been spooked by the new political landscape — seeing more hurdles to data centers under Democratic governors in key states, along with a Democratic-controlled Congress.

The poster child for debt stress is neocloud Coreweave. The yield on its 2032 bond with a 9.625% coupon has climbed above 16%. Coreweave's five-year credit default swaps spiked above 1,289 basis points on Thursday, widening above their October peak, according to Deutsche Bank. The CDS eased 8% Friday to around 1,155.

"We've had plenty of warnings from the credit space," said Jim Reid, Deutsche Bank's global head of macro and thematic research, in a note on Friday. Debt and equities are now moving more in sync, he added, "so we'll have to see if credit goes any further."

Investors are demanding more compensation to insure against default among even companies with the strongest balance sheets and credit ratings, including Nvidia, Broadcom, and Amazon.com.

CDS on Nvidia debt is up 76% in the last month. Broadcom's CDS is up 49%, while Amazon's has gained 44%. Their CDS remain low on an absolute basis; Nvidia's spread stood at roughly 139 basis points, compared with about 298 bps for Oracle and roughly 1,100 for CoreWeave. A large increase from a low starting point does not imply the same credit risk.

Still, rising CDS are a sign that markets want more protection against default among even the largest and healthiest debt issuers. And rising CDS imply higher financing costs down the line.

The market is now grappling with the prospect that at least some data center spending will be delayed or scrapped under incoming Democratic governors in Ohio, Texas, and Georgia, the three states with much of the planned capacity.

Moratoriums could impact 45% of planned capacity in the U.S. with a large amount of that in Texas, according to Goldman Sachs. Texas governor-elect Gina Hinojosa has said she's not opposed to data centers but wants a moratorium until the state legislature passes protections for communities.

None of these means AI debt financing is collapsing. Goldman tallies five AI bond deals totaling $67 billion since October 29, pushing total hyperscaler debt issuance this year to $296 billion. Goldman forecasts $440 billion of hyperscaler debt issuance next year, plus an additional $300 billion of project-level issuance across investment-grade and high-yield.

The Trump administration is vowing to support data centers. National Economic Council Director Kevin Hassett told CNBC on Friday that he expects data center growth to continue as forecast, despite Democrats' big election wins.

The big question now is how quickly AI spending will generate enough cash for hyperscalers to keep investors comfortable with future capex plans. For the neoclouds, any sign of consolidation or retrenchment in capex could result in fewer or shorter contracts for data center compute or delays in prepayments.

Goldman analysts said hyperscalers collectively reported negative free cash flow in the third quarter as capital spending exceeded operating cash flow.

"Delayed monetization and growth in purchase commitments—which we view as additional and sizable uses of cash, separate from Capex—are the key risks to more debt issuance, subject to market conditions," according to the bank.

Peak AI debt issuance may now be closer at hand. Bank of America analysts estimates that AI-related credit issuance will rise 31% from 2026 to 2027, hitting $685 billion, including public and private debt. They then see it falling 23% to $531 billion in 2028.

As for the risk? "Downside to these forecasts exists from a potential slowdown in AI infrastructure projects on the back of the Blue Wave, which will reduce capex needs and thus debt market funding," BofA said in a note.

-CNBC's Nick Wells and Michael Bloom contributed reporting

#2865. Montana State football commit breaks Idaho high school career yards record

Titus Vidlak has another Idaho state high school football record to his name.

On Friday, Nov. 6, Vidlak broke Idaho's career yardage record, leading Fruitland (ID) to a 45-22 win over Emmett (ID) in the IDHSAA Class 4A quarterfinals.

Vidlak finished with 253 scrimmage yards, bringing his career total to 9,628 as a four-year starter for the Grizzlies. That breaks the all-time yardage record previously held by Tanner Magnum (Eagle High School), who finished his high school career with 9,599 career yards.

In addition to the career yardage record, Vidlak set the Idaho state record for career touchdowns on Sept. 18 in a 41–13 road win over Bonners Ferry (ID). He broke the record previously set by Keegan Duncan (106), who went on to play linebacker at Utah State and win Idaho's Gatorade Player of the Year in 2018.

Following Friday's game, Vidlak now has 118 career touchdowns.

It was a dominant night for the 2027 Montana State football commit from the opening kickoff. After leading Fruitland to a touchdown on the opening drive, he returned a 60-yard punt for a score to give the Grizzlies the early 16-0 advantage early on.

Vidlak finished the night 9-of-12 passing for 110 yards and one touchdown, while also adding 14 rushes for 143 yards and three rushing scores.

On the season, Vidlak has now completed 98-of-139 passes for 1,266 yards, six touchdowns and two interceptions. He has also added 111 rushes for 1,060 yards and nine touchdowns.

Fruitland will take on No. 1 seed Homeland (ID) in the IDHSAA Class 4A semifinals on Friday, Nov. 13.

#2863. Stats Rundown: 3 numbers to know as the Mavericks wallop the Brooklyn Nets, 130-93

The Dallas Mavericks (6-4) defeated the Brooklyn Nets (2-8), 130-93 in a Sunday evening matchup at the American Airlines Center. The win over the Nets marks the Mavericks' second straight win and follows Friday night's 112-100 win over the Utah Jazz in NBA Cup Group Play.

Dereck Lively, Tarik Biberovic, and Sergio de Larrea were out for Dallas. Egor Demin and Mikel Brown Jr. were notably absent for Brooklyn.

Cooper Flagg led the Mavericks with 25 points and 12 assists. Kyrie Irving added 21 points. Michael Porter Jr. contributed 19 points for the Nets.

Here are three numbers to know from Sunday evening's win.

The Mavericks shot efficiently from the floor this evening, making 14-of-35 shots from behind the arc. The Mavericks are a better team when they are hitting their threes and not trying to force them up. Though against a less-talented and less-experienced team, the Mavericks won both the field goal and three-point percentage advantages. Dallas took care of a team that was a scheduled win and will need to carry their efficient night from the field into their next matchup.

Daniel Gafford had an active showing for the Mavericks, finishing with 18 points, three offensive rebounds, and a season-high three blocks. Gafford's ability to finish at the rim at an efficient rate and his shot-blocking abilities have allowed him to have a nice start to the season as the Mavericks' starting center. He has improved his efficiency this season and built on-court chemistry with Flagg, scoring on five of his assists for 10 points this evening. Gafford has been able to impact the game in a way he once did for the previous iteration of the Mavericks, allowing the Mavericks to stay afloat at the center position as Lively continues to rehab from his injury.

After an outing last week that served as a reminder that Flagg is still 19 years old, Flagg finished with an emphatic 25 points, 5 rebounds, and a career-high 12 assists. Notably, he shot 8-of-13 from the field in the win over the Nets, an improvement from his 17-of-54 (31.5%) field goals made in the Mavericks' three games last week. He played more decisively tonight on offense, highlighting his passing abilities and shooting 3-of-5 from behind the arc. Though the Nets are a borderline G League team, Flagg was able to completely dismantle their defense. When Flagg is playing confidently, he is one of the league's best players, and tonight's outing will give him the motivation to carry his performance into Monday night's matchup with Indiana.

Try not to miss them for too long; the Mavericks will be back in action Monday night as they host the Pacers. Tune in to KFAA-TV and Mavs TV at 7 p.m. CT to catch the matchup.

#2859. Lawsuit is filed over shirtless Bill Belichick video

On Saturday, former Patriots coach Bill Belichick returned to New England, for a game between North Carolina and Connecticut. One day earlier, a lawsuit over a leaked video of a shirtless Belichick was filed in Massachusetts.

Via Edward Lewis of the California Post, Jordon Hudson (who either is dating or was dating Belichick) sued the owners of an Airbnb rental for disclosing the Ring camera video generated three years ago, when Hudson and Belichick were using the property.

The legal argument seems to be that the owners, by disclosing the video of Belichick sans shirt, triggered a storm of unflattering scrutiny of Hudson.

From the lawsuit: "As a result of this leak, numerous false tabloid-like and click-bait stories have, and continue to, be published about her, which have held her up to intense ridicule and derision and caused immense damage."

The entire premise of the lawsuit seems to be that the leak "outed [Hudson's] romantic relationship with Belichick, something she had worked hard to keep private." One of the basic factual questions will be whether she behaved in a manner consistent with keeping her identity as Belichick's secret girlfriend private.

At some point, she wasn't keeping it private. She was flaunting it.

By filing the lawsuit, Hudson has pulled the pin on a grenade that may eventually throw shrapnel at Belichick. If, as it appears, she believes that she never would have gotten her 15 minutes (and then some) of infamy without the video of shirtless Bill being leaked, it will be fair for the defendants' lawyers to aggressively explore all of the ways in which she seemingly sought out attention and/or notoriety.

The lawsuit ultimately sets the stage for a potentially aggressive discovery process that will most likely include lengthy depositions of Hudson and Belichick.

And, during the lawyer-controlled question-and-answer sessions, Hudson will not be able to blurt out, "We're not answering that."

As it relates to Belichick, the lawsuit creates potential hassle, headache, and distraction. He should have tried to talk her out of doing it.

Maybe he did. If he did, he failed miserably.

#2858. How long will Rams’ Terrance Ferguson be sidelined?

Terrance Ferguson had a setback in practice last week and the details coming out are that Ferguson is expected to miss a few weeks. Will Ferguson be able to stay healthy enough to play again for the Los Angeles Rams in 2026? The expectation is, yes, but he was expected to play in Week 9 and that didn't happen. That's concerning.

Add in that Puka Nacua could miss Week 10, and if there is no Ferguson, the Rams may not have a reliable pass catcher. Will the Rams make a trade before Tuesday's deadline? If so, is there a receiver who can join and truly help the Rams less than a week later against the Arizona Cardinals?

The Rams may have to roll with who they have, and after Nacua, the Rams passing game is a question mark, and that's not a great look halfway into the NFL season.

""Pulled his hamstring. You can't make it up, can you?" McVay said, visibly disappointed. "He was running just a standard out-cut, felt his hamstring pull and probably be another couple weeks for him."

The Rams play the Cardinals next week, a game that Ferguson is almost certain to miss. After that, they have a bye in Week 11 before returning to face the Packers in Week 12 on Thanksgiving Eve. The Rams are going to take it a week at a time with Ferguson, as McVay wants to avoid any scenario where he isn't activated before the 21-day window closes."

""I thought our guys did a great job of being able to find a way," coach Sean McVay said. "There was a lot of opportunities that we missed offensively to make plays, extend drives, but they did enough. I love the way that Matthew responded after the turnover where he leads us down the field — had a bunch of gutsy conversions."

"He was awesome," McVay said. "That's one of the things that we want to be able to show as coaches, is show confidence to come right back to you. He made a bunch of plays. We needed all 14 of those catches."

"Stafford's first-half interception captured it perfectly. It was a poor decision to let that one go, but his frustration is understandable. The offense is predictable. It's not going to work in the long term.

Schefter added that the Rams don't believe he suffered a torn ACL, but the MRI will give them more information.

In the Rams' 20-13 win, Nacua caught 14 of his 19 targets for 150 yards and a touchdown. He also had two carries for 20 yards."

"Garrett finished with three sacks, a forced fumble, three tackles for loss and three quarterback hits in his first game since Week 1. His 93.7 grade led the Rams, and he had eight pressures on 37 snaps, according to PFF.

#2857. Player Grades: Recapping the Mavericks’ 130-93 win over the Brooklyn Nets

The Dallas Mavericks (6-4) took on the Brooklyn Nets (2-8), winning 130-93 on the first night of a back-to-back. This may be a game where one could draw correlations that aren't really there, or it may be the perfect example of a lesson learned. The Mavs slept on the Kings a few nights ago and got embarrassed, but tonight they came out strong and stayed that way, holding a lead for roughly 47 minutes.

Irving displayed his typical veteran poise, recognized his matchup against an inexperienced player and took advantage while staying within himself and doing what was best for the team. His three-ball left a bit to be desired (2-of-7), but there were times it felt like he was literally working on that part of his game in real time, as if to work out a kink. More importantly, he finished well at the rim with no hesitation getting amongst the trees; hopefully that is a sign of building confidence and there will be more of it to come. Despite the misses from downtown, Irving still shot 9-of-15 overall (60%). His circus shot for the and-one to end the third quarter was the cherry on top.

Flagg led all scorers in his double-double effort. He hit 8-of-13 from the floor, 3-of-5 from downtown and 6-of-6 from the free throw line. Three turnovers was about the only blemish on his game. In relatively few minutes, he all but dominated every aspect of the game and set a new career high in assists in less than 30 minutes of action.

Martin insists on being the most difficult player on the team to grade. Since getting the starting job a few games ago, Martin hasn't blown up the box score but has done a lot of intangible things. He's smart, lets the game come to him and finds teammates (even if it comes by way of the hockey-assist or poking a rebound to a teammate that doesn't register for him on the stat sheet).

This was a tough grade to give, as it's not especially high despite Washington having a pretty solid night. He was 2-of-3 from the floor, both from deep and did little bits here and there to contribute. It felt like this was a game he could go gang-busters in, especially off a great outing last game, but he was instead quietly effective.

Gafford was a mixed bag early on, with some strong finishes through contact for the and-one, but some matador defense on the other end. Then all of sudden he locked in and you blinked and realized he put together a very nice game. All said, he scored nearly a point per minute on a highly efficient 7-of-8 shooting from the floor while connecting on 4-of-6 free throws. His presence early on was a big help in the Mavs getting early separation.

May Clayton go down as a super slick pickup by the front office. The guy is totally in control, makes good plays and seems to be in the right spot all the time. In just 16 minutes he was terrific, boosting his grade given how little time he had to work. He drained 2-of-3 shots including 1-of-2 from beyond the arc. Two turnovers and three fouls hurt him, but he gets the benefit of the doubt relative to his role and minutes played.

He gets a major grade boost for how he managed himself throughout some chippiness with Noah Clowney. Clowney hit the deck after Johnson boxed him out, then proceeded to jaw with Johnson and bump him on their way back down court. Clowney persisted, bodying up into Johnson for an intentional foul, but literally bounced off Johnson for all the good it did. Johnson never got flustered, never retaliated and even though he was assessed with a technical foul, he showed poise well beyond his years. Also, Johnson made a heads up play that stood out in the first quarter. Instead of hopelessly closing out on an open shooter (he gave it a thought), he instead dropped to the basket to get a body on a cutter and ended up with the rebound. It was a savvy play he wasn't necessarily making a few weeks ago.

Aldama was 5-of-7 from the floor and 2-of-4 from deep. He barely played a quarter, but came in scorching. In the flow of watching the game live, it was hard to remember him getting so many shots – he was totally within the flow of the offense and got a couple easy ones right at the rim. It's a small sample size, but Aldama has thus far been everything Mavs' fans could hope for. Another smart player that doesn't get ahead of himself and makes the absolute most of his time on the floor.

Like Aldama, Risacher did a lot with a little. Aside from an air-balled three that he had to put up after getting the 'live grenade' tossed to him, he was on fire, shooting 5-of-8 (1-of-3 from downtown). He didn't commit a foul and pursued rebounds including two on the offensive end. He joined Aldama as a big reason the starters got a lot of rest.

This was a fantastic game all around. One could argue the Mavs played a struggling team. While that is true, we saw what happened against Sacramento just a few nights ago. Sunday night, Dallas got out to a lead, extended it and maintained it. The latter may have been the best part – the Mavs never relented and it seems to be in no small part due to Dusty May's coaching. May at one point (with a large double-digit lead) was still yelling to his players to box out. Anyone could have mailed it in on the first night of a back-to-back, but no one did.

I invite you to follow me @_80MPH on X, and check back often at Mavs Moneyball for all the latest on the Dallas Mavericks.

#2855. Mahomes And Kelce Are The Greatest Duo Ever

Patrick Mahomes and Travis Kelce have reached a new level in NFL history, and the reaction from fans proves the point. The Chiefs beat the New York Jets 35-12 on Sunday, Nov. 8, at Arrowhead Stadium to secure a big bounce-back win. Earlier setbacks against the Seattle Seahawks and Denver Broncos left the team at 5-2 prior to this commanding display. Even after years of success, both players are still performing at a high level without showing signs of slowing down. A third fan insisted, "They are not 'one of'… they are THE best duo of all time!"

Taylor Swift watched the game from her suite with friend Karlie Kloss and family members, wearing a Chiefs-red Tommy Hilfiger sweater. Mahomes also overcame a scare in the first half when he hurt his right arm near the elbow region. After speaking with trainers and testing his arm on the sideline, he returned to play the rest of the game. "If I'm able to play, I'm going to play. That's part of the game," Mahomes said, according to KCTV. He finished with 242 yards and two scores to help the team get back in the win column. #2854. Bears vs. Buccaneers: Inside the Snap Counts, Stats, and a Familiar Win

If you're a long-time fan of the Chicago Bears, then this 27 to 24 win over the Tampa Bay Buccaneers must have felt familiar. The offense kept shooting itself in the foot, so the defense had to bail them out, and then the special teams won it in the end.

The Bears looked unstoppable early, but after jumping out to a 14-point lead, everything that could go wrong did go wrong. But this team found a way to win, and if it felt like "the Bears are who we thought they were" type of game, you'd be right. The Bears became just the second team in NFL history to win a primetime game despite throwing at least four interceptions and rushing for fewer than 75 yards. The first team to do it was the 2006 Chicago Bears against the Cardinals in the "the Bears are who we thought they were" game.

Last night the Bucs had more total yards (352 to 349), more first downs (18 to 15), ran more plays (64 to 60), and had the time-of-possession edge (32:52 to 27:08). They also had fewer penalties, with 8 for 45 yards to 9 for 74 for Chicago.

The Bears did get the better of the Bucs on third downs as Tampa Bay was just 2-12 (16.7%) to Chicago's 7-13 (53.8%).

Tampa Bay's record may not look it, but that's a good football team that just happened to run into a better one on Sunday night.

This nugget from CBS Sports' Doug Clawson shows just how good the Bucs have been playing. "The Bucs are the 1st team with 1,000+ rush yards and 17+ takeaways in a 5-game span since the 1985 Bears."

And this nugget from OptaSTATS shows how much the Bears had to overcome to get the victory, as they are the only team in NFL history to have 5+ turnovers, 9+ penalties, have no 10+ yard rushes, blow a 14+ point lead, and yet still win the game.

Let's look at the Bears' playing-time breakdown and a few individual stats from the game.

This was one of the worst games of Caleb Williams' career, and the first time he ever threw four interceptions in a game. After the game, he immediately recalled that he threw three picks once in high school and threw three in one game in college. Considering he's one of the least-intercepted quarterbacks in league history, I'll view this stinker as an anomaly. He'll learn from it and come out after the bye focused, playing like the same Caleb we've gotten used to.

Despite the poor performance, he did lead the offense down the field on a game-winning drive, which was his first this year after a league-leading 6 a season ago.

Statistically, Jeffery Simmons had a "quieter" game than his debut, but he still filled the stat sheet, and his presence allowed his teammates to make plays. Simmons plays with a high motor (and he only missed 2 snaps), and that's infectious to the entire unit. If Simmons can chase wide receiver Emeka Egbuka 20 yards downfield to make a tackle, then every other defender can play with that same kind of effort.

Devin Bush had a season-high in tackles and passes defended.

Grady Jarrett and Shemar Turner picked up their first sacks of the year. Turner's was his first career sack.

Montez Sweat had his most tackles in a game since he had 7 on October 26, 2025.

Cairo Santos was 3 for 3 on extra points and made both field goals, including the 38-yard game-winner.

Tory Taylor punted 4 times for a 40.3-yard average, and he had 1 blocked for a second straight game.

Zavion Thomas had a 29.3-yard average of the 3 kickoffs that counted.

Kalif Raymond had 6 punt returns for 48 yards and 1 fumble that he recovered.

Roschon Johnson had 2 special teams tackles.

To check out the complete Bears vs. Bucs box score, I find ESPN's site is easy to navigate.

All statistics and snap counts, as well as the accompanying pictures, are taken directly from the NFL's Game Statistic and Information System.

#2852. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2850. Houston Rockets vs. Denver Nuggets game preview

The Denver Nuggets played in Mexico two nights ago. They beat the Indiana Pacers 114-102. With the extra level of travel, Denver has listed their entire rotation as a game-time decision. More than likely, some of them will have to play. However, if they do sit everyone, the Houston Rockets will owe them a huge debt of gratitude.

Houston has looked poor, to stay the least, over the past two games. Against Minnesota at home and in Phoenix, the Rockets got off to sluggish starts and it cost them in the fourth quarters when they couldn't score. Turnovers have also plagued the team in those losses. It was a stark reminder of last season's struggles, and casts Houston's hot start in doubt. Further, it brings into question the perceived importance of Fred VanVleet. If the team is making the same mistakes it made last season (sans FVV), how helpful is he?

The sky isn't falling, but boy do the Rockets need to prove that they aren't the exact same team as last season. Because if they are, what are we even doing here? I'm not arguing for a full teardown and I'm certainly not saying that a loss tonight should start that process, but if Denver doesn't play their stars and Houston still finds a way to lose, well…it's going to get loud.

#2848. Chris Jones: I lied when I said sacks don't matter, sacks are everything

The Chiefs came into Sunday's game against the Jets with nine sacks on the season and that didn't stop them from winning five of their first seven games, but it did lead to a lot of questions about whether the pass rush would come to life.

Defensive tackle Chris Jones responded to those questions after Week 8 by saying that sacks are overrated and that stats can sometimes be deceptive in regard to how the unit is performing across the board. Jones had a different answer after Sunday's 35-12 win over the Jets.

The Chiefs had five sacks in the game and Jones had two of them after managing just a half-sack in the team's first seven contests. That led him to change his tune about their value in the postgame press conference.

"Listen, I know last week, I was like, 'Sacks don't matter,'" Jones said. "I lied. Sacks are everything."

The Chiefs will take wins any way they get them, but Sunday was a good example of how sacks can pile up when you're playing from in front. They had one sack while building a 14-12 halftime lead and then teed off on Geno Smith once they were able to force the Jets into passing mode more often. The Chiefs tend toward closer shaves and going the opposite way on Sunday paid off for Jones and the entire defensive front.

#2847. Jaylen Brown doesn't expect to talk to Jayson Tatum in first game against Celtics since trade, feud

Don't expect to see Jaylen Brown and Jayson Tatum talking on Tuesday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

Brown is still very much trying to just put his chapter with the Boston Celtics, and his ensuing rift with Tatum, behind him.

"It's nothing to talk about. I mean, he's on his side, I'm on my side," Brown said on Sunday, via ESPN's Kris Rhim. "Just playing basketball. Both of us are trying to win, so nothing to talk about. Just check the ball up."

Brown was traded to the 76ers this past summer in a blockbuster deal that resulted in Paul George going to the Celtics. Brown's representatives insisted he never demanded to be traded, either, something that many had speculated was the case.

Brown spent a decade in Boston and nine seasons playing alongside Tatum — including one in which they won a championship. While the two appeared to get along just fine on the court, their relationship has seemingly ended in the wake of the trade.

Their "feud," if you can call it that, picked back up last month when Brown posted a blue hat emoji on Instagram in response to a video that Tatum posted. In that video, Tatum claimed he was also caught off guard by Brown's trade to Philadelphia. Tatum, Brown suggested by his cap emoji, was lying.

Tatum later downplayed the beef, and insisted that it "ain't no f***ing Shaq and Kobe" situation. Brown followed suit a day later.

"We've accomplished some great things. He's a hell of a basketball player," Brown said. "He's got a great PR team. There's no beef, but it might be a little lamb chops and some gravy. He should stay ready at all times."

Regardless of how things are between the two of them, their NBA careers are moving on in different directions. Tuesday's contest in Philadelphia marks the first between the two teams this season. Tatum has averaged 27.9 points and eight assists per game this fall while leading Boston to an 8-2 record.

Brown has averaged 21.6 points and 6.7 rebounds per game with the 76ers. They sit at 9-2 after a 127-121 win over the Chicago Bulls on Sunday. Brown had 29 points and seven rebounds at the United Center on Sunday.

While their first contest going up against each other feels like it may be an emotional situation for both of them, Brown isn't thinking ahead like that. At least, not yet.

"We'll see how I feel when we get out there, but as of now, I got no emotion towards anything," Brown said on Sunday. "So I'm just ready to go."
