#2843. Miles Russell joins Phil Mickelson as only 3-time AJGA Player of the Year winners

Miles Russell is joining select company with his latest golf accomplishment.

The Jacksonville Beach native was named the American Junior Golf Association Rolex Boys Player of the Year on Nov. 9, the third time he has achieved the honor from the nation's largest junior golf tour.

Here's the list of three-time AJGA Player of the Year winners:

That's the list. Russell adds the 2026 award to his 2023 and 2025 honors from the AJGA.

Asterisk Talley of Chowchilla, Calif., is the AJGA Girls Player of the Year. Russell and Talley will be honored alongside the 2026 Rolex Junior All-America teams and the Rolex Scholastic Junior All-America team at the Rolex Junior All-America Awards Banquet on Nov. 21 at the JW Marriott San Antonio Hill Country Resort & Spa in San Antonio, Texas.

"Their talent, perseverance and sportsmanship reflect the very best of the AJGA," said Stephen Hamblin, AJGA Executive Director, in a statement.

"It's quite an honor to win an award of that prestige three times," Russell told the Times-Union. "It's pretty special. But again, all I like to do is play golf, and luckily I can play some good golf."

Good may be understating it. Russell, who will begin playing at Florida State in the fall of 2027, finished the year first on the AJGA boys' rankings and is fifth on the World Amateur Rankings.

His best finishes in junior events in 2026 were a victory at the Sage Valley Invitational (becoming the first repeat winner) and a second in the U.S. Junior Amateur.

Russell has six career AJGA victories, highlighted by the 2023 and 2025 Junior Players Championship (he was the youngest ever to win and the first First Coast resident to win in 2023, then became the first two-time winner two years later) and nine junior national titles in all

Russell reached the semifinals of the Western Amateur, qualified for match play in the U.S. Amateur and was the youngest member of the U.S. Walker Cup team, where he went 2-1-1 at Lahinch in Ireland. Russell also played for the U.S. in the Junior Presidents Cup and went 2-1 to help the U.S. win at Medinah.

Russell also made the cut and tied for 39th in the U.S. Open at Shinnecock, after winning a playoff in a 36-hole sectional qualifier, tied for 15th in the Korn Ferry Tour's event in Savannah, Ga., and made his third career cut in a PGA Tour event with a tie for 64th in the Butterfield Bermuda Open.

Russell, who turned 18 years old on Nov. 1, said the AJGA played a vital role in his development.

"It's been a huge part of the game and it's a very good stepping stone to get to the next level," he said. "It prepares you for amateur golf, and amateur golf helps you prepare for professional golf."

Russell said he will take the time during the holidays to work on all facets of his game.

"I'm working on everything, from golf swing to my driver to chipping and putting," he said. "If I can just try to keep improving my irons, everything will keep improving. It's not just one thing, but there are things here and there that we like to target. Golf's a streaky game. Some days my driver's really good, my putting's not. Some days my putting's really good, my driver's not quite there. So really just trying to improve and be as consistent as possible."

Russell said he will play a similar schedule in 2027 as he has in the past several years, combining high-level junior and amateur events with Korn Ferry Tour and PGA Tour opportunities through Monday qualifiers or sponsor invitations.

"We'll try to get into as many [pro events] as we can," he said. "Just try to be there, show up, and play good golf."

#2839. Who won Jessie Bates III trade? Grading Jaguars, Falcons in deal

One of the most notable moves ahead of the NFL trade deadline hinged on a player who wasn't widely known to be on the block.

On Monday, the Jacksonville Jaguars agreed to acquire former Pro Bowl safety Jessie Bates III from the Atlanta Falcons in exchange for a 2027 second-round pick and 2028 fifth-round pick, according to multiple reports.

The swap helps an already stingy Jacksonville defense shore up one of its most glaring weaknesses while boosting its big-play quotient. The Falcons, meanwhile, prioritize the long-term outlook of their roster while saying goodbye to one of the roster's more reliable performers.

But who won the deal? Here are grades for each team:

On its face, this seems like a pretty exorbitant price tag for a soon-to-be 30-year-old safety on the final year of his deal.

This, however, feels like another instance of Jaguars general manager James Gladstone's calculated aggression bubbling to the surface. It's easy to see the Los Angeles Rams influence of his background in such a move.

Defensive coordinator Anthony Campanile's unit has made its name off creating havoc for opponents. The Jaguars rank second in scoring defense (15.1 points allowed per game), thanks in large part to their consistent ability to generate takeaways and stuff the run. But when unable to generate pressure or pick off opposing quarterbacks, Jacksonville's track record is a bit more dicey. And the defense ranks 27th on EPA per play allowed on passes more than 20 yards downfield, according to Next Gen Stats.

It's easy to see why Gladstone would see these trend lines – which only figured to worsen after Eric Murray's season-ending elbow injury – and took action. The Jaguars might be able to run away with the AFC South, but they couldn't expect to face the likes of Josh Allen, Joe Burrow and Patrick Mahomes in the playoffs and avoid having that vulnerability routinely exploited.

Will Bates provide enough of a boost? It's difficult to say. He's still a shrewd presence in coverage, but he doesn't exactly change the entire complexion of the back end.

The cost is ultimately too difficult to stomach, but this is the kind of move a contender makes to capitalize on a Super Bowl-ready roster.

More: 32 things we learned in NFL Week 9: QB crises testing teams as backups flounder

Kudos to Matt Ryan and Ian Cunningham for not stubbornly barreling ahead with a roster that remains firmly in the mix for the NFC South crown.

Standing pat would have been the easy route, and perhaps the one that provided the best odds for ending an eight-year playoff drought that owner Arthur Blank desperately wants to see ended.

But Atlanta's new leadership clearly understands the scope of the reset it's staring down. Getting such a rich return for a player who likely wasn't in the franchise's long-term plans is nothing short of laudable.

Projected to have an NFL-high $110 million in cap space in 2027, according to Over The Cap, the Falcons couldn't have counted on a compensatory draft pick in 2028 had they let Bates walk in free agency. Instead, Atlanta locks in some significant assets right away.

Perhaps defensive coordinator Jeff Ulbrich can prove as resourceful in trying to compensate for this loss as he was in masking the absences of Jalon Walker and James Pearce Jr. for a short-handed pass rush. And in the NFC South, the Falcons still might have enough to get by.

Still, it's difficult to see how a secondary that got roasted by Burrow in Munich might be able to regroup down the stretch without one of its most reliable presences.

The important thing, however, is that Atlanta is now much better positioned for a leap in 2027 and beyond.

#2835. Eagles star opens up about trade rumors and maybe leaving Philly: ‘I’m sad’

PHILADELPHIA — Eagles tight end Dallas Goedert knows there's a chance that he played his last game with the team on Sunday afternoon, but he doesn't want to get moved.

"I love this city," Goedert told reporters after the Eagles' 19-11 loss to the Giants at Lincoln Financial Field. "I've always dreamt of retiring here. So we'll see what happens."

Ahead of the NFL trade deadline on Tuesday, Nov. 10, the 2-7 Eagles could retool their roster by trading players with expiring contracts for future draft picks.

Goedert re-signed with the Eagles on a one-year deal last offseason to come back for his ninth season with the team. Unless the Eagles are planning to re-sign Goedert for a 10th season, trading him for a Day 3 pick before he leaves this offseason would make sense.

If the Eagles trade Goedert, second-round rookie Eli Stowers would receive more snaps during the final eight games of the season. Stowers could grow into a starting-caliber tight end before next season.

Goedert, who was once one of the best tight ends in the NFL, is still a solid starter. He has recorded 26 catches for 258 yards and two touchdowns this season. In a more productive offense, he might put up better stats.

"I talked to [Eagles general manager] Howie [Roseman] briefly a couple weeks ago, and we had a good conversation," Goedert said about his future. "Obviously, anything can change, and for the right price, obviously, everybody's available. So we'll see what happens. But not too worried about it."

Goedert snagged five catches for 50 yards against the Giants' defense, the second-most of any Eagles player. He had some good moments, but Philadelphia's passing attack struggled once again with a new quarterback in the lineup. Joe Milton recorded 18 completions for 169 passing yards.

If Goedert doesn't get traded, he wants to help fix Philadelphia's struggling offense, which has been one of the worst units in the NFL through the first nine weeks of the season.

"Losing sucks," Goedert said. "I hate losing. But we still get to play football. We still got a great group of guys in this room that we get to come to work with and try to pull ourselves out. So we'll get to see how everybody takes it. But I'm ready to come into work and I'm ready to do whatever I can to help this team try to win games in the future."

#2834. Karl-Anthony Towns’ renewed commitment has Knicks defense off to fast start

Continuity works in different ways. For the Knicks, it's been a positive for their defense.

Three weeks into their title defense, the Knicks are among the NBA's stingiest teams while allowing 104.2 points per game. That was third best in the league heading into Sunday's games. It's partially a product of New York playing at the slowest pace in the NBA, resulting in fewer points for both teams.

But the Knicks were also ranked seventh in defensive rating — calculated by points allowed per 100 possessions — which is the same position they finished last season.

Evan Mobley #4 of the Cleveland Cavaliers shoots over Karl-Anthony Towns #32 of the New York Knicks, Saturday, Nov. 7, 2026, in Cleveland. Getty Images

The difference this time around? It took the Knicks three months of terrible defense last season to start climbing to No. 7. They were 19th in the NBA through Jan. 19, then caught fire and were No. 2 in defense for the final 43 games.

Already in this campaign, they're thwarting opponents at a top-tier rate. They obviously have OG Anunoby, a premier and versatile individual defender. But in today's NBA — where ball handlers hunt switches and favorable matchups through screens — a defense is often only as good as its weakest link.

And Karl-Anthony Towns, who has been criticized throughout his career for lazy defense, has been far from weak. Much like during his strong playoff run, KAT has been active off the ball, committed and clearly in shape after cutting weight in the summer.

During Saturday's win over the Cavs, Towns grabbed 13 rebounds and had two steals — including a strip of Donovan Mitchell on the perimeter late in the third quarter. Towns stepped into Mitchell's driving lane on a screen, established position and didn't let the All-Star split the two defenders. Mitchell coughed up the ball as he fell to the court, which led to a Towns assist on Landry Shamet's 3-pointer to cap the game-breaking 25-12 Knicks run.

Towns said his experience defending Mitchell — they were Western Conference opponents for five years — provided him the foresight to see the split attempt coming.

"That comes with familiarity. That just comes with playing each other so much," Towns said. "You know how great he is when he splits. If he gets that split over, he's most likely dunking on somebody.

"So you don't want to be the cause of that. So just having that experience and just knowing them very well … With a lot of experience and time in this league, you see a lot of different offensive sets and things. So just utilizing that experience and being in the right spot and putting yourself in the right position for your teammates."

Knicks coach Mike Brown lauded Towns' basketball IQ and credited his two defensive coordinators — assistants Darren Erman and Brendan O'Connor — for putting the center in the right positions.

New York Knicks' Karl-Anthony Towns (32) grabs a rebound against the Cleveland Cavaliers in Cleveland, Saturday, Nov. 7, 2026. AP Photo/Phil Long

"KAT's got a tremendous feel. He's a smart dude when it comes to a lot of things, but especially when it comes to the game of basketball," Brown said. "His feel is at a high level, and so you couple that with the job that [O'Connor] and Erman are doing, it's easy to see where he is because everything we've asked him to do, he's tried to do.

"So if you're extremely coachable, you got a great feel and you got two guys in Brendan O'Connor and Darren Erman that are teaching you the game defensively at a really high level, you'll see the results we're seeing now."

Brown had challenged KAT on defense, with the positive results really kicking in during the playoffs.

"It's [Towns]. It's his willingness to do what's best for the team to continue to get better," Jalen Brunson said. "I think we've all been challenged by Mike in some way, shape or form. And he's stepped up to the plate with that. And he's been great."

Not coincidentally, so has the Knicks' overall defense. They've held opponents to 105 points or fewer in five of the past six games. They've also won five of their last six. Also not a coincidence.

#2831. Bills at Vikings Monday Night Football betting odds, picks and predictions: Best bets, player props

We've got a cliche combo for Monday Night Football.

First, the Bills' irresistible force of an offense takes on the immovable object that is the Vikings defense.

Second, in the deepest pessimistic recesses of sports evaluation, you might have heard someone say "they're one injury away from being a good team."

In college football, due to modern financial commitments to recruits and transfers, teams stick with an underperformer at a key position because of what they've invested in that player. Then, when they finally give up, turning away from a quarterback they've committed to, it seems like half the time the replacement does a better job. That's how little anyone knows about the pool of players.

In the NFL, usually the drop-off is massive between what a starting quarterback makes and their backup — in the tens of millions of dollars annually. That's not the case for the Vikings' salary matrix, as Kyler Murray accounts for a cap-hit of just over a million dollars, and Carson Wentz actually makes more, at $3 million.

So, when Murray was ruled out of Monday night's game with a hamstring, and the betting line progressively shifted from Bills -3.5 down to -3, it made economic sense, at least.

By individual game market rating, the Bills are the highest rated team in the NFL (about a half-point better than the 49ers). Meaning, on a neutral site, there's no team they wouldn't be a favorite against.

At 6-1 overall and 5-2 against the spread, that might not seem all that controversial for the Bills, but it's an unusual status for a team in the bottom-five of the league in defensive metrics like adjusted Weighted EPA/Play and Success Rate.

The Vikings are also 6-1, and depending on the number you grade them on in Week 7, they're 5-2 ATS as well, but while bettors respect defense, they love a team that can score. So, Minnesota never got near the power rating of Buffalo's, even while undefeated, and the Vikings have taken a hit in perception after losing their first game last week.

In Week 2, the Vikings were actually rated as a below-average team when Wentz started in the rain in Chicago. While the conditions were terrible for efficient quarterback play, adding his relief appearance in Week 1, Wentz has the 22nd-best EPA/Play, compared to Murray's 35th (out 46 qualifying quarterbacks), and Wentz has been in Kevin O'Connell's offense for two seasons now.

With an opening line of Bills -3.5, oddsmakers were essentially ready to say "enough is enough" when it comes to Murray, having just seen him struggle in Detroit, often frustrating star receiver Justin Jefferson.

Murray had a chance to get the Vikings offense going against the Dolphins, Colts, and Lions, but couldn't do it. Against the Bills, Wentz will have the opportunity to take the QB1 job, and it should function as well as it has all season.

Meanwhile, like most quarterbacks, Josh Allen's numbers decrease against the blitz, and that's what you can expect from Brian Flores' unit.

In the end, you've got a great offense and bad defense travelling to take on a great defense and an offense that is capable of at least being average. Except the former is rated at the top of the league, while the latter is considered just better than mediocre, and should also have a best-in-class home-field advantage.

Josh Allen under 20.5 pass completions (-125, FanDuel)

Like a game of "Battleship," if you call the right number, big plays can be found against the Vikings defense, catching them on a coverage break on an all-out blitz. If Allen strikes on a few of those, he won't need to accumulate pass completions, in the same way Jordan Love and Baker Mayfield clicked on exactly 50% of their pass attempts when facing this unit.

The Lions used a strategy of running right at Minnesota with 34 total carries between Jahmyr Gibbs and Sione Vaki.

Of course, Allen could use his legs to try to make the Vikings pay for not getting all the way home, as well.

Add in that the Vikings could have some decent time of possession against the Bills defense, and we can only envision Allen going over this number if he's compelled to throw close to 40 times — an unlikely game script.

Ty Johnson over 14.5 receiving yards (-115, Bet365)

If the Vikings defense is doing its thing, the Bills will face a ton of third downs, many in passing situations. That's where they turn to Ty Johnson in the quick-game.

Johnson has caught all 15 of his targets from Allen this season, often finding his way to a double-digit reception because either teams aren't aware he's on the field and dangerous, or they're playing off the line of scrimmage.

The Vikings line up at the line of scrimmage and often retreat, so the Bills should look to catch them backpedalling by using Johnson out of the backfield.

Carson Wentz over 209.5 passing yards (-113, DraftKings)

Minnesota could win convincingly, but when they do it's because the defense stalls out the opposing offense. The Vikings offense isn't likely to push out to a lead and feel like they can shut things down. Wentz should be able to connect on a few deep shots against the Bills, and the pressure of seeing Allen on the other side should keep the urgency up late, going for the kill shot to keep the ball out of his hands.

ORCHARD PARK, NEW YORK - NOVEMBER 01: Josh Allen #17 of the Buffalo Bills jogs onto the field through the tunnel prior to a game against the Baltimore Ravens at Highmark Stadium on November 01, 2026 in Orchard Park, New York.

An anytime touchdown bet on Josh Allen, at this price, in each game of the season, would have grossed six units to just 1.25 lost, as Allen has already tumbled into the end zone nine times in seven games.

With 14 last year, you would think that the betting market would be aware that he's the Bills' primary goal-line option, but his season-long rushing touchdown line was 10.5. That's all you need to know about how much the betting market has underbought Allen's scoring prowess — and still hasn't adjusted.

From further away from the goal line, the Bills may need to win some 1-on-1 matchups on the outside — and that's the role Coleman plays in this offense.

In all but two games this season, Coleman has led the receiving corps in snaps, as the big threat on "go" routes, which is in keeping with the hope that he becomes the true No. 1 he was projected to be. With a touchdown in three straight games before last week, Coleman's odds are too long, even against this defense.

You can build a little long-shot portfolio with Coleman, Johnson and a deep pull from the Vikings roster below, but with Johnson's explosiveness in the pass-game out of the backfield, he needs to be included at these odds.

You could tell the squeaky wheel was getting the grease last week in Detroit, as Kevin O'Connell pushed Murray towards Justin Jefferson in the end zone. However, because of Murray's inaccuracy, it took a circus catch to get Jefferson's just his second touchdown since Week 1 (when he caught two from Wentz).

With a comfy matchup against the Bills' secondary, and a price that still doubts Jefferson's status as one of the greats in the game (because of bad QB play the last two seasons), we'll go with Minnesota's most likely scorer.

If you think Jefferson has had issues connecting with Murray, within the constraints of the Vikings offense, you don't know the half of it. Literally. Addison's only caught half of the balls thrown his way this season (to Jefferson's 70%).

If the Minnesota offense finds its way against the Bills, the receiver across from Jefferson should get some favorable matchups, and Addison is a dangerous long-ball threat if the deepest safety gets too interested in what Jefferson's doing.

Back when the Vikings' offense worked, and even last season when it was a similar struggle, the secondary tight ends (Josh Oliver, Johnny Mundt) often found their number called off of O'Connell's red-zone playsheet, with nine touchdowns over the last two years.

This year, with so few trips near the goal line, there's been less opportunity for misdirection. Plus, Oliver's on IR.

Ben Yurosek, in his second year out of Georgia, missed the first four games of the season, but in the three he's played, he's been on the field for half the snaps, and has a catch of 10 and 24 yards. He's worth finishing off our long shot TD portfolio for a few dollars at better than 10-to-1.

You can find more valuable betting analysis from Yahoo Sports betting contributor, Matt Russell, at THE WINDOW.

#2826. Ex-Celtics Guard Reveals Who He Blames for Jayson Tatum-Jaylen Brown Fallout

Ex-Celtics Guard Reveals Who He Blames for Jayson Tatum-Jaylen Brown Fallout originally appeared on NESN. Add NESN as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Marcus Smart believes the media played a major role in driving a wedge between Jayson Tatum and Jaylen Brown.

Ahead of Tuesday's game between the Boston Celtics and Philadelphia 76ers, Smart offered his perspective on Tatum and Brown's relationship in an interview with The Athletic.

"For them to end how it is, I blame a lot of it on the media," Smart said. "Because when I was there, we were good, we were rocking. When I left, they were still good and rocking. I think they just allowed certain stuff in the media to come in between them, to create that gap.

"Everybody put assumptions on the guys. Saying they're this, saying they're that, creating a wedge, creating a villain, creating a hero. That (factors into it) a lot."

Smart has a unique perspective on Tatum and Brown's relationship. He spent six seasons alongside the duo in Boston, watching them develop into two of the NBA's biggest stars.

The Celtics achieved plenty of success during Smart's tenure, but they also experienced their share of heartbreak. Smart was in Boston for Gordon Hayward's horrific ankle injury, multiple Game 7 losses and the 2022 NBA Finals defeat to the Golden State Warriors.

Smart was traded to the Memphis Grizzlies in the 2023 offseason. Tatum and Brown went on to lead the Celtics to an NBA championship in 2023-24.

Smart expressed hope that his former teammates can eventually put their differences aside. He admitted it's been "tough to see" two players he considers his brothers for life reach this point.

#2825. What Cooper Flagg and his emotional support PB&J say about the Mavericks’ chances in the West

Cooper Flagg has been playing angry in the Dallas Mavericks' last two wins. But at least the team has prevented the second-year star from getting hangry. You wouldn't like Flagg when he's hangry.

Late in both Friday's 112-100 win over the Utah Jazz and in Sunday's 130-93 blowout over the Brooklyn Nets, Flagg could be seen on the bench as things wound down, snacking on a peanut butter and jelly sandwich.

And leave it to the intrepid Dallas sports media apparatus — when they spot a trend, they pounce with all the aggression of a rabid dog. No angle goes unnoticed around here.

So, naturally, Flagg answered questions about his emotional support PB&J after Sunday's win. He gave an enthusiastic nod as the question was being asked about a "second straight in-game PB&J."

"Listen, I want to give a shoutout to our strength staff, our medical staff," Flagg said. "They're the best in the business. I'm trying to put on some weight — keep gaining muscle, so there are some requirements I've got to meet, so I'm just trying to stay ahead of it. We've got another game [on Monday], so just trying to get my calories, stay ahead of it and be ready for another one tomorrow."

He also confirmed that the jelly in question was grape flavored, and the peanut butter was of the smooth variety. Let's get real. He's not a savage.

So let's break this down. Depending on weight, minutes played and how aggressively they move on the court, an NBA player can burn 900-1,800 calories over the course of a single game. Larger, heavier players (like Flagg, despite your opinion of the second-year star's frame at 6'10", 228lbs) burn toward the higher end of that range.

As such, sports dietitians generally advise heavier full-time athletes to consume anywhere from 4,000 to more than 7,000 calories per day. One peanut butter and jelly sandwich on white bread can account for somewhere in the neighborhood of 350 to 500 calories.

Flagg was listed at 205lbs entering his rookie season. He's now listed at 228lbs. Considering all the full-court basketball he's played in the meantime, that's one hell of a lot of PB&Js along the way.

So, what does Flagg's effort to make weight say about the Mavericks' chances in a suddenly wide open Western Conference? That he sees an opening, and even in the early part of a breakout second season, he's actively adding to his game because he doesn't see himself as a finished product.

Fans may be looking for Flagg to take the next step with his outside shot, and for the record, he made 3-of-5 from 3-point range on Sunday against the Nets, but he's working on those GAINS. Flagg has increased his scoring (in an admittedly small sample size) so far this year from just over 21 points per game to just over 23 points per game. He's largely accomplished that by getting to the free-throw line a ton more.

Last season, he shot just over four free throws per game. This year, he's getting to the free-throw line more than seven times per game. In one of the Mavericks' first games of the year, color commentator Devin Harris said he'd like to see Flagg increase his free-throw rate to somewhere in the neighborhood of 8.5 per game, which would put him near the top of the league, if you're looking at last year's leaders in that category.

Getting stronger is always a great idea, especially when you're 6'10." Flagg has already shown an increased ability to bang inside through the first 10 games of the year. Being able to go both over and through defenders in the lane will increase Flagg's ability to finish inside, which will, in turn, improve the team's efficiency in the lane.

In a Western Conference that looks more wide open this season, every little advantage gained becomes a bigger deal.

#2816. Wolves 115, Jazz 100: Yet Another Complete Performance

It wasn't always pretty, but the Minnesota Timberwolves found a way to push through and defeat the Utah Jazz on Sunday night.

Every time the Wolves would go on a bit of a run, the Jazz would claw their way right back into the game. It was not until late in the third quarter that Minnesota would comfortably pull away. This allowed the Wolves to salvage their showing against a team that came into the game at 1-8.

I've mentioned this in my recaps before, but in the past, a game like this could be considered a trap game. Playing against a team that is languishing in the standings is usually a recipe for disaster. Early on in the season, it would seem that Minnesota is doing a great job of rewriting that narrative.

The Wolves did what they were supposed to do by beating a team at the bottom of the standings. We shouldn't take that for granted. I doubt any long-term fans will.

The victory over Utah was on-brand for the 2026-27 Timberwolves, featuring a slew of highlights and standout performances from many of the players who have been ascending in the absence of Anthony Edwards and Donte Divincenzo. Here are a few game notes that stood out to me.

I've been apprehensive about saying that Trey Lyles is going to be a meaningful part of this team. I mean, the guy was overseas last year. Yet, as is becoming tradition, Minnesota is looking like the premier rehabilitation hub for players who are looking for newfound life in their careers. Lyles is fitting right in.

The counting stats don't jump off the page tonight with just nine points (3/6 shooting, 1/3 from deep), six boards, two assists, and a steal across 21 minutes of play. What jumps out instead is his +29 across those limited minutes in what amounted to a 15-point win. The guy can play, and it's fair to wonder at this point why he wasn't in the NBA last year.

Many folks I have talked to have branded Lyles as the team's new Kyle Anderson, and even that comparison may be underselling his impact at this point in the season. Lyles is connecting lineup combinations in a way that we never saw with Anderson. He fits into any scheme at any point of the game, and he is rarely a negative on the floor, whereas Slow Mo needed a very specific set of circumstances to succeed.

On nights like tonight, when Jonathan Kuminga has an off performance (four points, three turnovers, and a team-worst -10), having a consistent presence like Lyles proves to be invaluable. Tim Connelly can apparently do no wrong with his roster construction, and Lyles is further emblematic of his prowess as a team builder.

Against a (lowly) team like the Jazz, you need your bona fide contributors to not drop the ball. The quartet of Jaden McDaniels, Rudy Gobert, Ayo Dosunmu, and LaMelo Ball were all up to the task, keeping the Wolves afloat against a Jazz team that showcased more fight than their record would suggest.

What more can we say about Dosunmu? He's been revelatory in the starting lineup and, much like Lyles, seems to fit in and produce wherever he's deployed. Scoring 26 points on just 13 shots is wildly efficient and, while his five turnovers were tied for the most on the team, he seemed to be unstoppable for most of the night.

It's wild to think that Connelly acquired Dosunmu for Rob Dillingham, Leonard Miller, and a few second-round picks. Peanuts.

Ball was his usual self on the evening, providing plenty of highlight-worthy plays, some dazzling shot-making, and even throwing in a breakaway dunk for good measure. He shot 50% from the field on 20 shots, which is always a plus for a player who tends to struggle with efficiency. Not much to complain about on his end.

McDaniels was also efficient and filled the stat sheet on a small shot diet: 16 points (5/9 in general, 2/3 from deep), six boards, three assists, a steal, and a block, and a +17 across 33 minutes. Jaden did what he does, which is contribute in all facets of the game. Gobert had a whopping 15 rebounds, 10 points, and only missed two free throws. All around, it was a solid showing from the gang.

TJ Shannon Jr. also returned from his injury absence with a modest five-point performance. He may need some time to reacclimate himself to game speed. Fortunately, the Wolves have a bit of a break before their next matchup (Thursday night against the Dallas Mavericks), so he can continue to rest his injured hip.

In an otherwise uneventful game, it was refreshing to hear Michael Grady back on the call.

Obviously, Grady has increasing commitments that take him away from doing Wolves games. That's all good, and nobody deserves it more than he does. However, it is always nice to hear his chemistry with Jim Petersen shine during the home broadcasts.

The joy was palpable early on in this game, and it is easy to tell how much Grady still loves calling Wolves games. His national broadcasts are similarly fantastic, though the degree to which he feels at home with Minnesota brings a warm feeling to the game-watching experience that we, as fans, shouldn't take for granted.

None of this is a knock on Kevin Calabro, who has been able to provide a different angle to the watching experience that will only improve the more that his chemistry grows with Petersen. Calabro is good and is exactly as advertised. Yet, Grady is, and will always be, our dude. While he's still with us, we need to soak this up while we can.

The Timberwolves get a bit of a break after a torrid start to the season, taking on the Mavericks on Thursday, November 12th at 7:00 PM CT. Catch the game on DAZN-MIN and NBA TV.

#2814. FIA boss planning to end age limits for office holders

FIA president Mohammed Ben Sulayem is planning to end age limits for office holders in motorsport's governing body.

The move, which has been proposed for approval at next month's FIA General Assembly, comes six months after Ben Sulayem ended term limits for those holding leadership posts across the organisation.

The FIA statutes currently rule out anyone over the age of 75 from standing as part of a presidential team.

Critics say it is the latest step by Ben Sulayem as he seeks to ensure he can remain FIA president for life.

The proposal to be put forward to the General Assembly, which has been seen by BBC Sport, argues that "age limits could restrict the number of qualified candidates eligible for election by the competent bodies, in particular individuals whose experience and expertise could be of benefit to the FIA".

An age limit of 70 was imposed in 2023, during Ben Sulayem's first presidential term.

It was a tactic to prevent MotorsportUK boss David Richards from being able to oppose Ben Sulayem at the 2025 presidential election.

At the time, Ben Sulayem, who is 64, argued that this would "enhance governance and accountability within the organisation".

The FIA added that the move to introduce an age limit "reflected the FIA's commitment to maintaining high standards in its leadership and governance structures".

The FIA has been contacted for comment on the change of stance but has not provided a response.

Ben Sulayem's move comes in the context of a European Parliament resolution passed in October 2025 on "the role of EU policies in shaping the European Sport Model".

This includes a clause that "recognises that the autonomy of sport is conditional upon good governance, and calls on international, European, and national sports organisations, as well as stakeholder-representative organisations, to implement the highest standards of governance, integrity and transparency, with appropriate checks and balances to ensure accountability".

A consultation on the topic, launched in April 2026, is due to report by the end of this year.

The EU's aim is to "tackle the major challenges facing the sport sector. These include financial sustainability and inequalities, governance issues, volunteering decline and inclusion. The goal is to address these challenges through coordinated action at European level."

The FIA age limit was raised to 75 in 2024 on the basis that it was "modernising the governance of the FIA and ensuring that candidates for the presidency are more aligned with current leadership practices".

Age limits for leaders are not universally common in sports governing bodies. Some implement them to promote renewal, others choose not to, allowing the widest breadth of candidates.

Ben Sulayem was first elected FIA president in 2021 and was re-elected unopposed last December as a consequence of a quirk of the FIA statutes preventing any other candidate from standing.

Three people who wanted to oppose him - including former racing driver Laura Villars and ex-FIA steward Tim Mayer - were blocked from doing so.

Villars is currently engaged in a legal action against Ben Sulayem and the FIA in France challenging its electoral process.

Ben Sulayem's presidency has been marked by a series of controversies on the issues of governance and transparency.

In addition to the removal of term limits, he has introduced statutes reducing accountability and concentrating power in his hands.

And he has fired a series of senior officers who questioned aspects to do with his leadership, including:

the former chief executive officer Natalie Robyn who raised questions about the general governance of the FIA

the head of the audit committee Bertrand Badre, a former head of the World Bank, and the compliance officer who investigated and cleared Ben Sulayem on charges of interfering with two separate aspects of F1 operations

Ben Sulayem's first vice-president for sport, Robert Reid, resigned in April 2025 citing "a fundamental breakdown in governance standards within" the FIA.

At the General Assembly, to be held in Shanghai, Ben Sulayem faces a request by the Ukraine Automobile Federation (FAU) to include on the agenda the issue of violations of the FIA statutes and international sporting code by the Russian Automobile Federation (RAF).

The RAF has opened regional branches in four cities recognised as sovereign areas of Ukraine by the United Nations.

The FAU has already asked the FIA to look into the issue through its court of appeal but has received no response.

The FAU is asking the FIA to suspend the RAF's membership of the international body until it closes all its offices in Ukraine and suspends all activity there.

FAU president Oleksander Feldman said: "Our proposal is fully consistent with the position of the International Olympic Committee regarding the conditions for the participation of Russian athletes in competitions.

"Indeed, such participation became possible specifically on the basis of guarantees provided by the Russian Olympic Committee that it would not conduct activities on the sovereign territory of Ukraine."

#2808. Lakers vs. Hawks Preview: Streaking teams collide

The Lakers (9-2) will continue their current five-game road trip in Atlanta where they will take on the Hawks (7-4) on Monday. This is the first matchup between the teams this season.

Who: Los Angeles Lakers vs. Atlanta Hawks

After taking down the league's top-ranked defense in the Pistons on Sunday, the Lakers have little time to celebrate as they're back on the court less than 24 hours later for a matchup in Atlanta. Both teams enter this meeting in good form with each looking to extend their win streaks.

The Lakers have strung together five in a row while the Hawks are not too far behind with four. One streak will end on Monday.

Los Angeles will have the rest disadvantage in this one after going down the wire with the Pistons today. But in fairness to the purple and gold, they've been doing well on the second leg of their back-to-back games this young season, winning two so far.

The biggest question mark entering this is whether Luka Dončić will suit up after he was sidelined with back tightness on Sunday. If the current MVP favorite does, then that would obviously be huge for the Lakers. Dončić can give the Hawks' defense trouble like no other and that pressure will help free up the Lakers' offense.

Speaking of the Lakers' offense, it would really help if they knock down their shots in this one. The Hawks are a mediocre 3-point shooting team and have struggled to contain opposing shooters. They have Dyson Daniels and Nickeil Alexander-Walker to create havoc and disrupt passing lanes which is why it'll be ideal if the team can contribute all-around instead of rallying on Dončić and Austin Reaves, who is coming off a 43-point game.

The Hawks have two dynamic and pesky guards in CJ McCollum and Alexander-Walker, so expect the Lakers' perimeter defense to have its hands full. Remember, this isn't the same Atlanta team since Trae Young departed. They're a confident squad that plays commendable team basketball and at a much faster pace than the Lakers. Jalen Johnson might just be a matchup problem for the Lakers. Look for Atlanta to double down on that on Monday.

The Lakers' grit has been one of the many enjoyable aspects of watching this team so far this season. It's become a defining trait and hopefully we see it again versus the Hawks on Monday. Let's see if the Lakers will be the team that walks away with their winning streak still intact.

Since the Lakers played on Sunday, the injury report for this game will only be released hours before tipoff.

It's worth noting, though, that Quentin Grimes hurt his finger against the Pistons, so his status might be questionable for this one.

For the Hawks' injury report, Keshon Gilbert (left knee inflammation), Mouhamed Gueye (left foot fracture), Asa Newell (left great toe hyperextension), Henri Veesaar (right ACL tear) are out.

Jock Landale (left hip strain) is questionable.

#2807. Sunday Night Football: Bears overcome five giveaways, beat Bucs 27-24

It was a wild one in Chicago, but the Bears were able to pull out a 27-24 victory over the Buccaneers and move to 7-2 on the season.

Caleb Williams and the Bears’ offense started out white hot, but an interception midway through the second quarter threw off the unit for nearly the rest of the night. Williams ended up tossing four interceptions — the most in a single game he’s had in his college or pro career.

But even with five giveaways — Luther Burden also had a fumble in the fourth quarter — the Buccaneers scored just 10 points off of them. Tampa Bay couldn’t turn a blocked punt into points either, despite getting the ball at midfield.

Chicago began the game with a 14-0 lead, as Williams tossed touchdowns to Rome Odunze and Burden. The Buccaneers got on the board with Chase McLaughlin’s 27-yard field goal in the second quarter before Cairo Santos hit from 25 yards to make the score 17-3 with 3:02 left in the half. McLaughlin drilled a 21-yard field goal to end the half, with Bucs head coach Todd Bowles explaining in his halftime interview on the NBC broadcast that he felt his team could score to start the third quarter.

That turned into reality, as Bucky Irving scored a 2-yard touchdown to cap Tampa Bay’s opening drive in the second half, narrowing Chicago’s lead to 17-13.

While Williams threw his fourth interception, Baker Mayfield then threw a pick of his own to Jaylon Johnson, who returned it 67 yards for a touchdown.

McLaughlin again made it a one-possession game with a 35-yard field goal with 11:24 in the fourth quarter. Then Burden’s fumble gave the Buccaneers the opportunity to tie it deep in Bears territory, with Irving scoring on a 6-yard run. Mayfield made the two-point conversion successful by scrambling up the middle to the end zone.

But even with the offensive challenges, the Bears got the play they needed to win it. Williams connected with Colton Loveland for a 38-yard pass all the way down to the Tampa Bay 16.

After running time off the clock, Santos sent the ball through the uprights as the fourth quarter expired for a 38-yard, game-winning field goal.

Williams finished the contest 22-of-37 for 279 yards with two touchdowns and four picks. Burden led with seven catches for 97 yards with a TD. He had to be examined in the sideline medical tent after his fumble in the fourth quarter and was subsequently taken back to the locker room for further evaluation.

Mayfield finished 18-of-32 for 243 yards with two picks. Irving had 97 yards on 20 carries with two TDs, plus four catches for 28 yards.

With the win, the Bears are 7-2 in the league’s best division — as all teams in the NFC North have at least six wins. Chicago will be on its bye in Week 10 before hosting the Saints in Week 11.

At 3-6, the Buccaneers will also be on their bye in Week 10. They’ll look to get back on track on the road against the Lions in Week 11.

Billi Mucklow featured on The Only Way is Essex from 2011 to 2013, before returning briefly in 2018 [Getty Images]

The footballer denied the first offence when he appeared at court in March.

#2805. Ashton Jeanty had 5 catches for -5 yards on another awful day for Raiders' running backs

The Raiders’ running back situation has been awful all season, but it hit a new low in Sunday’s loss to the 49ers.

Ashton Jeanty, the running back the Raiders drafted with the sixth overall pick last year, had a historically bad game catching passes out of the back field, finishing with five catches for negative-five yards.

Yes, that’s negative yardage for the game. Raiders quarterback Kirk Cousins threw seven passes to Jeanty, and these were the results: incomplete, 3 yards, 3 yards, incomplete, -3 yards, -7 yards, -1 yard.

That’s the second-worst performance ever for a player with at least five catches in a game. The only player ever to finish with fewer yards was Jamie Caleb of the 1960 Browns, who somehow finished a game with five catches for -18 yards. It’s unclear how Caleb managed that feat, but Caleb finished his career with seven catches for -26 yards, so he was doing something spectacularly wrong when the ball was thrown to him.

And “spectacularly wrong” describes everything about the Raiders’ running backs this season. In Sunday’s loss, Jeanty was their leading rusher, with 25 yards on six carries. The Raiders’ other running back, Mike Washington, had six carries for negative-three yards. Between the two of them, Jeanty and Washington had 17 touches for 17 yards. One yard per touch is pathetic.

For the season, the Raiders have a total of 222 rushing attempts for 642 yards, a horrendous average of 2.89 yards per carry. They’re the only team in the NFL averaging less than three yards per carry. Nothing is going right for the Raiders when the ball is in the running backs’ hands.

#2801. Malik Nabers: Fox tried to "create a narrative" about me being a bad teammate

Giants wide receiver Malik Nabers took to social media on Sunday night to express his displeasure with how he was portrayed during the Fox broadcast of his team’s 19-11 win over the Eagles.

In a post to his Instagram story that was later taken down, Nabers took particular issue with Greg Olsen’s characterization of him. After quarterback J.J. McCarthy threw a pass behind Nabers in the fourth quarter, Fox showed Nabers gesturing with his hands and Olsen said he was letting the quarterback know to put the ball in front of him. Play-by-play announcer Joe Davis said Nabers’s “body language has been terrible” throughout the game with Olsen agreeing that it wasn’t good.

The broadcast also showed Nabers slamming his helmet on the sideline at one point and sideline reporter Pam Oliver discussed Nabers’s desire to have the ball come his way. Olsen went on to say that there is “a fine line between being demanding” and being worried about your own touches that it detracts from the success of others. Nabers responded to what he believes was an implication that he’s a bad teammate.

“Y’all be trying [to] create a narrative on me soo bad, it’s actually crazy!! If you [don’t] like the way I play the game, keep the camera off me!! SIMPLE! But [Olsen] calling me a bad teammate when every team I’ve been on, there’s NOBODY I’ve played with has said dat bout me is crazy! My face the same way if I’m havin a good game, bad, winning or losing,” Nabers wrote. “If it’s a problem. 1. Keep the camera off of me. 2. Pray bout it! Simple.”

Nabers finished the game with five catches for 43 yards, which snapped a four-game streak with at least 111 receiving yards. He said in his postgame media session that he was frustrated with how the offense played at points in Philadelphia, but his frustration with Olsen and Davis might have been even greater.

#2798. Austin Reaves leads Lakers past Pistons with Luka Doncic out

Austin Reaves and the Los Angeles Lakers earned a 121-116 victory over the Detroit Pistons at Little Caesars Arena on Sunday, Nov. 8.

The Lakers improved to 9-2 on the season and remain in first place in the Western Conference. The team also moved to 2-0 in games in which Luka Doncic does not play.

Doncic has positiioned himself as the early favorite for the NBA's MVP after leading the league in scoring and averaging 40.1 points per game.

The point guard was ruled out with lower back tightness, allowing for more scoring opportunities for Reaves.

"I was a lucky basketball player tonight," Reaves told reporters after the game.

Reaves produced a game-high 43 points on 14-of-27 shooting from the field, against one of the league's best defenses. He also went 4-for-6 from the three-point line and a perfect 11-for-11 from the free-throw line.

The Pistons were not surprised by Reaves' abilities, having pursued the shooting guard with strong interest during free agency in the offseason.

"Luka is an incredible player, and I think there are times when he's playing that A.R. kind of has to take a secondary role," Lakers center Walker Kessler told reporters after the game. "But you could see tonight that he's a very capable player.

Kessler contributed to the victory, putting together a double-double performance with 12 points and 16 rebounds.

The Lakers offense as a unit also created a fast start and built a strong lead in the first quarter, outscoring the Pistons 38-14.

The Lakers will travel to play the Atlanta Hawks on Monday, Nov. 9 in the second game of a back-to-back at 7 p.m. ET.

#2797. NFL Mock Draft 2027: Eagles land Jeremiah Smith

The Eagles' playoffs odds are bordering on an impossibility at this point. Barring some epic collapses from Philly's NFC East rivals (possible) combined with the greatest turnaround you've ever seen from the Eagles offense (absolute fantasy), Howie Roseman should be looking ahead to the offseason. As it currently stands, the Eagles hold the fifth overall pick in the 2027 NFL Draft. I would be shocked if they pick any later than that at this point and their chances of getting a better pick feel pretty high right now.

Here is a midseason simulation of the first three rounds using Pro Football Focus' Mock Simulator. While a ton will change between now and late April, this is a good way for us to familiarize ourselves with some prospects to get excited about.

The biggest question going into the Eagles offseason is what happens with the quarterback position. Can Jalen Hurts play football again? Does he want to play football again? Does he want to play football for the Eagles? If the answer to all of these questions is (miraculously) yes, then the Eagles should double down on supplying Hurts with as many weapons as possible. Jeremiah Smith is the best wide receiver prospect since Julio Jones and AJ Green came out of college in 2011. There are no holes in his game and he would immediately be one of the best receivers in the NFL as a rookie. Now imagine him as WR1 with DeVonta Smith on the other side and Makai Lemon in the slot. That is a great way to get Hurts (or any other quarterback) cooking.

The turnover at offensive line this season has been a mess. Injury luck combined with youth at key positions has led to the offensive line being a real problem. Greg Johnson has been a stellar player for the Golden Gophers over the last few years. He has great size and strength, with the ability to thrive at guard or center. He'd be an immediate upgrade for Philly on the inside at all three positions he'd be able to play.

One of the many frustrations this season has been the Eagles lack of edge pressure. While Nolan Smith is having a solid year, the rest of the Eagles edge guys have been really mediocre, at best. Boubacar Traore is a bit of a pass rush specialist, at 6'5" and 250 pounds. He has great length and burst. Coming off a dominant game against Miami, Traore is getting some real NFL hype now. Maybe he goes a lot higher than this, but right now this would be a steal for the talented pass rusher.

#2796. Carl Granderson: Saints defense was disrespected, so we came in ready to play

Browns quarterback Shedeur Sanders’s response to questions about the Saints defense leading into Sunday’s game drew the ire of Saints linebacker Carl Granderson and they remained on his mind after a 23-22 overtime win.

Granderson said before the game that the team would have a “chip on our shoulder” because of the smile that Sanders flashed when asked what he’d seen from the New Orleans defense. Granderson had a couple of sacks in the win and then opened his postgame press session by responding to a question about how much motivation the unit drew from Sanders.

“I mean, we seen a lot, you know, on film, so I do not think it was motivation. We saw them chirping,” Granderson said. “So we just came into this game ready to play. I think it is disrespectful for somebody to laugh at our defense, you know, take us as a joke. So we went out there and balled out. They had 2.8 yards in carry, and we stopped the run at 95 rushing yards on 34 attempts. So we did our thing on defense, and in overtime, the two-point conversion. So that was a good team win on defense.”

Sanders had his moments and the Browns would have won with a successful two-point play after a touchdown pass to tight end Harold Fannin in overtime. That would have likely led to a different response from Granderson, but the Saints made one last stop and they were able to get the last laugh as a result.

#2795. One Braves Link Shows Smart Pitching Approach

The Atlanta Braves were reportedly linked to right-hander Casey Mize at the 2026 Trade Deadline, but the team wisely kept its distance. According to Gabe Burns of the Atlanta Journal-Constitution, the organization was "prominently linked" to the free agent pitcher during the summer months. However, Burns noted that Atlanta's actual interest in Mize was "overstated" by the media chatter.

This hesitation makes sense for a front office that values health and production. Mize posted a 7.42 ERA across 44.2 innings after his move to the San Diego Padres, according to Battery Power. That performance stood in sharp contrast to his time in Detroit earlier in the year, where he held a 2.70 ERA over 86.2 innings. The drop in numbers was severe, with his FIP jumping from 2.58 to 7.17 in his final stretch.

The Braves have seen enough rental players crash and burn to know better than to chase a mirage. Mize's fWAR fell from a solid 2.9 with the Tigers to a negative -0.7 with the Padres. A change of scenery clearly hurt his production, and the team had no reason to bet on a rebound without proof. The front office also held injury concerns regarding his health history, which adds another layer of risk to any potential deal.

Passing on Mize allows the club to focus on targets who fit their long-term plans. The organization prefers players who can stay on the field and deliver consistent innings. Chasing a pitcher with a rocky recent track record and a history of time on the injured list does not align with building a deep rotation. The decision to step back shows a clear understanding of the player's true value.

Health is a major concern for any team looking to add depth. Championship-hopeful teams face concerns due to Mize's significant time on the IL in the 2026 season. The report mentioned that Atlanta had injury concerns regarding his fellow target, Nick Pivetta, as well. Pivetta made only eight starts in 2026 before a right flexor strain ended his season early.

These injury risks explain why the Braves did not pursue either pitcher aggressively. The front office knows that a healthy rotation wins games, while a shaky one loses them. The team's restraint in the face of rumors suggests they are looking for reliable arms rather than just names on a list. The team's search for reliable arms rather than just names on a list protects the organization from wasting resources on players who might not be able to contribute.

The Braves remain active in the search for pitching help, but they will likely wait for the right fit. Mize will have plenty of interest during the free agency cycle, but Atlanta may not be the team to sign him. The club will look for a deal that matches their style and avoids the pitfalls of a high-risk rental. The 2026 season showed that the team knows how to handle the market.

Bears wide receiver Luther Burden went for a medical evaluation near the end of Sunday night’s win over the Buccaneers and head coach Ben Johnson updated his condition after the game.

#2793. Patriots vs. Packers snap counts: Christian Barmore in the spotlight ahead of NFL trade deadline

Even coming out of a blowout loss to the Green Bay Packers and dropping to 5-4 on the year, the New England Patriots are not necessarily in "seller" territory heading toward Tuesday's NFL trade deadline. That being said, one player has still been mentioned as a potential candidate to be moved over the past few weeks: defensive tackle Christian Barmore.

Whether or not a trade will materialize remains to be seen, but Sunday's game did little to clear the smoke emanating from that particular fire. Barmore, after all, played a season-low 26 of 61 defensive snaps for a playing time share of 39%. While the margin is not significant — he averaged 31 snaps and a 50% share over his first seven games — it, at the very least, creates more data points to fuel speculation over the next 24-plus hours.

The other top-level DTs, after all, did not see such a drastic change relative to their normal usage. Milton Williams had a slightly lower playing time share than his season average up to that point, while Cory Durden was on the field a bit more.

Does that mean a trade is imminent? No, it doesn't.

Playing time fluctuates based on a variety of factors including opponent, game script and physical fitness. In Barmore's case, he did have some positive contributions by registering a sack and a stop on a 3rd-and-1 run. Of course, that probably still won't stop the rumor mill from turning until 4 p.m. ET on Tuesday.

#2792. Fantasy Basketball Top 200 Rankings: Historic heater pushes Luka Dončić into the top spot

Last week was an eventful one for elite performances, as some of the NBA's top players did what fantasy managers expect stars to do. Oklahoma City's Shai Gilgeous-Alexander dropped two 40-point stat lines on the Cavaliers in consecutive games, while San Antonio's Victor Wembanyama recorded a stat line in a win over Dallas that only Wilt Chamberlain and Bob McAdoo had before.

But if one player has truly been on fire, it's Lakers point guard Luka Dončić. He's recorded at least 35 points, five rebounds and five assists in each of his last seven appearances. Dončić has scored at least 40 points in four straight games, including a 53-point effort against the Trail Blazers on November 6. His averages during that four-game run: 45.0 points, 9.0 rebounds, 7.8 assists, 2.3 steals and 7.3 three-pointers per game while shooting 52.4 percent from the field, 45.5 percent from three and 87.0 percent from the foul line.

Thanks to this run, Dončić belongs in the conversation with Gilgeous-Alexander, Wembanyama, and Denver's Nikola Jokić for the most valuable player in fantasy basketball this season, regardless of league format. He did not play in the Lakers' November 8 win over the Pistons, and availability can be a concern for a player who last exceeded 65 games in 2023-24. However, that can't be used as an excuse not to acknowledge Dončić's production.

Week 4 Look Ahead: Luka Dončić continues to dominate, while Kon Knueppel is back on the injury report

Luka and Victor Wembanyama had elite performances in Week 3, while the Bulls welcome back center Nic Claxton.

G Darius Acuff Jr., Sacramento Kings: Over his last four games, Acuff averaged 25.3 points, 4.0 rebounds, 9.0 assists, 1.5 steals and 2.8 three-pointers while shooting 44.2 percent from the field and 82.4 percent from the foul line. In Sunday's loss to the Heat, he recorded his second 30-point double-double, finishing with 32 points and 10 assists.

C Brook Lopez, LA Clippers: Lopez has started the Clippers' last three games, scoring 20 points in each of the last two. The days of him being an elite fantasy center may be in the rearview mirror, but the 7-footer has proven more valuable than many managers expected before the season began.

G/F Klay Thompson, Miami Heat: Sometimes getting away from home can help a player lock in. Thompson scored at least 19 points in three of the first four games of Miami's five-game road trip, including a 30-point effort in a November 4 win over the Clippers that he capped with a buzzer-beating three. Also, Thompson has made at least seven three-pointers in two of his last three games.

G Payton Pritchard, Boston Celtics: Over the last week, he's shot 33.3 percent from the field while averaging 9.5 points per game. He did finish the week with three straight games in double figures, but fantasy managers expect more from the former NBA Sixth Man of the Year.

G/F Brandon Miller, Charlotte Hornets: In Charlotte's three games last week, Miller averaged 11.7 points, 2.3 rebounds, 2.0 assists and 1.3 three-pointers per game while shooting 27.9 percent from the field and 16.7 percent from three. With Kon Knueppel out for at least four weeks and Dennis Schröder (hamstring) out for at least two weeks, the Hornets need more from Miller. Unfortunately, he's dealing with an adductor injury and is day-to-day.

C Kel'el Ware, Milwaukee Bucks: Ware's inconsistency remains an issue, and he struggled throughout Milwaukee's four-game week. The 7-footer averaged 2.0 points, 4.3 rebounds and 1.0 blocks in 13.6 minutes per game, shooting 19.0 percent from the field. So much for Ware's ability to provide reliable fantasy value despite coming off the bench.

#2787. Baker Mayfield: Bears barked out the Buccaneers' cadence, causing a false start

The Buccaneers were flagged for five false starts on Sunday night, and quarterback Baker Mayfield said at least one of them was caused by an illegal tactic by the Bears.

Mayfield said left tackle Tristan Wirfs jumped when Bears players yelled Mayfield’s cadence, something that is a penalty when the officials notice it but is hard for the officials to know. Mayfield said he’s not sure what else the Bucs could have done.

“I don’t know,” Mayfield said. “The last one, I know they were barking out the cadence, which you can’t do that. It’s tough for the refs to call that because they’re not right there at the line of scrimmage. They called that on Tristan, but you can’t do that.”

That last false start Mayfield referenced came during a brutal stretch for the Bucs’ offense: Immediately after that false start, Mayfield was hit while throwing an incomplete pass, and on the next three plays Mayfield was sacked, although one of the sacks was wiped out by a penalty. The Bucs’ offensive line looked out of sorts, and Mayfield thinks the Bears’ cheating was a contributing factor.

#2786. Game Preview: San Antonio Spurs vs Phoenix Suns

Running through one of the weaker parts of their schedule, the Spurs have been stacking up wins while largely walloping the competition.

Barring a one-point win over the Mavericks in which the Spurs couldn't buy a shot from downtown, they've largely run roughshod over the competition, with 4 double-digit wins in the last five contests.

Next up on this list is a Suns team that gave the Spurs some real trouble early last season (splitting the season series) but has been pretty inconsistent out of the gate.

Last season, the Suns' deep roster made things difficult with a combination of shrewd passing, long-distance marksmanship, and sturdy defensive play.

The savvy passing (5th in assists) and shooting (12th in 3pt%, 8th in FG%) have remained hallmarks, but the defense and depth look rough without Grayson Allen and Royce O'Neale, who were traded to Charlotte for Miles Bridges, who is struggling to fill the void left by two important contributors.

The Suns are 27th in Defensive Rating, and 23rd in Net Rating, and while they're on a two-win streak themselves, the Spurs have been a buzzsaw, ranking 2nd in Offensive Rating, 3rd in Defensive Rating, and tied for 1st in Net Rating over the last eight games, narrow win over the Mavericks included.

Provided the Spurs take the game seriously and/or refrain from shooting 5-36 from three again, this should be another double-digit smack-down.

It certainly doesn't hurt that Wembanyama is averaging 37 points per game over the last three games on 60/40/80 shooting. Barring collective brain-farts, I like San Antonio in this one.

#2785. Rams expect Terrance Ferguson to miss "another couple weeks" with hamstring injury

The Rams planned to have tight end Terrance Ferguson come off of injured reserve to play against the Commanders in Week 9, but he wound up being ruled out after Friday’s practice.

Ferguson has been on injured reserve while recovering from an ankle injury and he suffered a hamstring injury at practice ahead of Sunday’s game. After the 20-13 Rams win, head coach Sean McVay said at his press conference that the injury will keep Ferguson out for a little more time.

“Pulled his hamstring. You can’t make it up, can you?” McVay said. “He was running just a standard out-cut, felt his hamstring pull and probably be another couple weeks for him.”

The Rams host the Cardinals in Week 10 and they have a bye in Week 11, so Ferguson will only miss one more game if McVay’s timeline is accurate. Even if it will take a little longer, Ferguson will have to be activated before his 21-day return window closes in order to avoid being shut down for the rest of the season.

#2781. 4 thoughts as the Mavericks look to stay hot at home against the Indiana Pacers

The Dallas Mavericks (6-4) have suddenly found some momentum again. Monday's game against the Indiana Pacers (3-7) gives the Mavericks a chance to keep it going on the second night of a back-to-back set against a team they've already beaten once. Dallas enters at 6-4 after blowing out the Brooklyn Nets 130-93 on Sunday, while Indiana comes to town at 3-7 after falling 114-102 to Denver on Saturday. The Pacers are still without Tyrese Haliburton (left hamstring), and Dallas should have a real opportunity to finish the homestand with a third straight win.

Here are three things the Mavericks need to do to make that happen.

Kyrie Irving has started to look much more like himself, and Dallas needs that to continue.

He scored 21 points against Brooklyn and looked comfortable getting to his spots without needing to dominate the ball. That balance is important. Dallas is at its best when Cooper Flagg controls possessions as a playmaker while Kyrie picks his spots, attacks mismatches, and gives the Mavericks a reliable scorer whenever the offense stalls.

The Pacers have some capable perimeter defenders, but this is still a matchup Kyrie should be able to attack. Indiana is already missing the guard who normally controls so much of its offense, and Dallas has a chance to put even more pressure on them if Kyrie gets going early.

The Mavericks do not need him taking 25 shots every night. They just need this version of Kyrie, efficient, aggressive, and capable of completely changing a game when Dallas needs a bucket.

Dallas already knows this matchup can work.

The Mavericks scored 44 points in the first quarter and took a 77-43 lead into halftime the first time these teams met before eventually winning 121-105. Flagg played only 17 minutes before leaving with a shoulder injury, but Dallas had already taken complete control of the game.

Indiana should be better prepared this time, but the same advantages remain. Without Haliburton, the Pacers lose the player who normally runs the offense and pushes the pace. Pascal Siakam remains their biggest threat, while Ivica Zubac gives them size inside, but Dallas should feel good about its ability to create better shots.

The Mavericks have to avoid assuming the first meeting means anything automatically, but if they move the ball and defend with the same energy, this should be another favorable matchup.

The best part of the Brooklyn win was how easily Dallas generated offense around the rim.

The Mavericks scored 64 points in the paint, with Flagg repeatedly getting downhill and finding cutters or Daniel Gafford once the defense collapsed. Gafford finished with 18 points, and Dallas looked much more dangerous offensively when it was creating inside-out rather than settling for jumpers.

Indiana has size, but Dallas has enough creators to keep putting pressure on the defense. Flagg getting into the middle of the floor opens everything else, especially when Washington and the guards are ready to shoot, and Gafford is available as a lob threat.

When Dallas gets consistent paint touches, the offense looks completely different.

Dallas played Sunday night and now has to turn around and play Monday again, while Indiana had Sunday off. The good news is the Brooklyn game was basically over early enough for Dusty May to avoid running his starters into the ground.

Flagg played fewer than 30 minutes, and the bench handled much of the fourth quarter.

That should help, but Dallas still cannot come out sleepwalking. Indiana has enough veteran talent to punish a flat start, and Siakam can carry the offense if Dallas lets him get comfortable.

The Mavericks have looked much better over the last few games. Monday is a chance to prove the momentum is real, not just the product of playing Utah and Brooklyn.

The Mavericks and the Pacers will tip at 7:30 p.m. on Monday from American Airlines Center. The game will be televised locally on KFAA Channel 29 and on sister stations throughout the Mavericks' regional viewership area. You can catch the stream on MavsTV or on NBA League Pass where available. The radio broadcast can be heard on KRLD 105.3 The Fan.

#2780. Report: Will McDonald requests a trade away from the Jets

The Jets have balked at the idea of trading running back Breece Hall or wide receiver Garrett Wilson during their latest losing season, but linebacker Will McDonald is hoping they’ll be amenable to shipping him out of town.

Ian Rapoport of NFL Media reports that McDonald has requested a trade ahead of Tuesday afternoon’s deadline. Per the report, teams have called the Jets about McDonald but they have not engaged in discussions about a deal to this point.

McDonald was a 2023 first-round pick and the Jets picked up his fifth-year option before this season. He’s set to have a $14.475 cap hit in 2027. After spending most of the last two seasons as a starter, he has been in a rotational role this season and has played less than half of the team’s defensive snaps.

McDonald has 16 tackles, three sacks, a forced fumble and two fumble recoveries this season. He has 24.5 career sacks and we’ll find out by Tuesday afternoon if he’ll be trying to add to that total in a new uniform.

#2779. Fantasy Football FanDuel Showdown Perfect Lineup: Justin Jefferson & Josh Allen Leads MNF DFS Breakdown

Josh Allen and the Bills bring an offensive juggernaut into Minnesota, but the Vikings have a defensive nightmare waiting for them on Monday night. With fantasy football upside scattered across both rosters, finding the right Captain and salary-saving plays could separate tournament winners from the rest of the field.

With playoff-caliber rosters colliding, roster construction for your FanDuel single-game DFS MNF showdown slate requires balancing elite high-ceiling tier options with value-driven salary relief.

Two heavyweights square off inside U.S. Bank Stadium as both teams enter this Week 9 showdown sitting at 6-1. The Bills have one one of the highest-scoring offenses in the NFL, driven by Josh Allen's ability to create off-script plays and score on the ground inside the red zone. On the other side, Minnesota's defense continues to bring relentless pressures and disguises, as Brian Flores' defense poses a real threat to high-volume passing performances.

With a tight game script expected, game stack construction will require balancing high-priced studs with value options capable of breaking through in single-game FanDuel Showdown formats.

Nov 1, 2026; Orchard Park, New York, USA; Buffalo Bills quarterback Josh Allen (17) reacts after the game against the Baltimore Ravens at New Highmark Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Gregory Fisher-Imagn Images | IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Josh Allen (QB, Buffalo): High-Volume Passing Ceiling Combined with High-Leverage Rushing Floor Inside the 5-Yard Line: Allen remains the ultimate focal point of any single-gam slate. As the engine of Buffalo's offense, he offers dual-threat floor and ceiling that few players can match, factoring into both passing and high-leverage red-zone rushing opportunities. Even in tough road environments, his ability to rack up explosive plays through the air and on the ground makes him the premier target for the MVP slot.

Allen remains the safest candidate as his dual-threat ability grants him multiple ways to finish as the top scorer on the slate.

Oct 25, 2026; Minneapolis, Minnesota, USA; Minnesota Vikings wide receiver Justin Jefferson (18) practices before the game against the against the against the Indianapolis Colts at U.S. Bank Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Jeffrey Becker-Imagn Images | IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Justin Jefferson (WR, Minnesota) Jefferson's target share ensures elite volume . If Minnesota falls behind or relies on the passing game, his ceiling matches any player on the slate. His elite route-running precision and yards-after-catch capability means he only needs a couple of broken tackles to smash his salary expectation.

James Cook (RB, Buffalo) A dual threat out of the backfield, Cook benefits from a high touch share, particularly in high-tempo schemes. His ability to catch passes cleanly out of the backfield elevates his baseline output on full-PPR and half-PPR formats like FanDuel. Minnesota's front seven will focus on containing Allen, which should create lighter boxes and clean creases for Cook on early downs.

Minnesota's front seven should focus heavily on containing Allen, which should create lighter boxes and cleaner creases for Cook on early downs.
